  rebinds to it (`rebound` in `sc downed`) - no new record, so no second "is down" tell and no second `ScBond.NoteDowned`.
  A body that loads standing (a new session's boot clear) is handled as before.
- **Dismembering kills (D1, no code).** 0-XNPCCore's `ShouldCatchKill` refuses a dismembering or disintegrating hit; SC's
  U2 transpiler swaps only `HasPlayerLeader`, so this applies to every SC person who can go down.
- **The re-down warning (D3, `ScDowned`).** 0-XNPCCore sets `SdcsDownedRedown` to its `RedownWindowSeconds` at a revive and
  counts it down while she is up; `FragileSeconds` reads it. `Up` tells `scDownedRevived(By)Fragile` with the minutes left
  (the plain keys when the window is 0), `FragileText` goes on the community window's status (row and detail,
  `XUiC_ScCommunity.StatusText`), and `ConsoleState` on `ScOrders.Describe` (`sc members`, `sc xnpc <id>`: DOWN or
  FRAGILE). SC has no look-at text for members, so nothing else changes.
- **Cornered healing (D2, `ScHealBackOff`, `CorneredHealBelow` 0.35).** With no room to back off and her health above the
  line, `StartWalk` marks the wait "fights on" (`FightOnWait`, no heal-now; `heal-cornered-fights-on`), `Wanted` stays
  false for `CorneredRecheckSeconds` (4 s) or until her health reaches the line, F4's gain clock restarts, and
  `ScMedic.HeldByReach`'s MedicMaxWait timeout waits (`CorneredFightsOn`). At or below the line: heal-now-cornered, as
  before. A walk that finds room clears it. Not cornered (room, the enemy keeping up, shelter, watch, Stay): unchanged.
- **The weapon toggle is SC's order (D4, `ScArms`).** Voice Control's `SetWeaponPref` writes `SdcsWeaponPref` (1 melee, 2
  ranged). `Patch_VcWeaponPref` (prefix + finalizer on `VCCommandRouter.MutateSetWeaponPref`) makes it SC's order from its
  argument (`NoteOrderKind`), and the swap inside it is no order of its own (`vcOrderScope`, `OnSwap`). `NoteOrderKind`
  writes the preference for SC's own orders (`SetPref`), `ScTeam.ClearOrders` clears it, `SyncOrderPref` (AfterPostInit)
  gives an older SC order its preference, and `OrderedMelee` / `OrderedRanged` read the preference when neither SC cvar is
  set. With the ranged preference 0-XNPCCore vetoes its own 3 m swap (`WeaponPreferenceRules.CloseMeleeVeto`), so R2's
  melee in `KiteHand` is SC's swap then (`TakeMelee` + `JoinBout`, `x5-cornered-pref-swap`).
- **Quiet kills.** `ScArms.Hand` swaps nothing while `UAITaskStealthKillSdcs` is the running task (`StealthKillRuns`, the
  type test in a NoInlining method; `x5-stealth-no-swap`). `Patch_VcStealthKill` (on `MutateStealthKill`) keeps its torch
  swap from becoming a melee order. `SdcsStealthTargetId` / `SdcsStealthOrderAt` are in `ScOrders.VoiceMovementCvars`
  (cleared at stationing). `ScOrders.Describe` names engage stance 3 "hunting" (Voice Control's SetEngage 4); the wheel's
  hunting and quiet kill are Voice Control's to answer, the toggle's label is `ack.swapweapon` (`ScVoiceRoutes.FamilyFor`).
- **SdcsLastRanged (X5's `SyncLastHeld`).** With the ranged preference 0-XNPCCore's world-entry step reads
  `SdcsLastRanged`; a ranged-tagged weapon in her hand is stamped there too.
- **Residents fight what is in reach (finding C, `Patches_VoiceControl.Patch_MayEngage`).** Voice Control's refusal becomes
  `RefusalPass` (0.85 since the nc1 review, was 1) for `EngagesAtWill` bodies, so MeleeAttack on the enemy in reach beats MeleeApproach toward
  a refused far one (vanilla's `UAIPackage.DecideAction` keeps the first strictly higher score, and MeleeApproach is
  listed first). Round nc3 (the user) removed nc1's second half - 0 beyond `DefendBaseRadius` for a resident at home
  (`UAIConsiderationScIntruder.BeyondDefendRadius`): it gated every shot of the ranged package, so a resident stopped
  shooting the zombies chasing the player the moment they followed the player out of the base. `BeyondDefendRadius` is
  unused now (H1 calls `RadiusWhy` itself); the walk is bounded by the leash (ScReturn, `PastLeash`): a melee-only
  resident can still go out to it (zone + `ResidentRoamZoneMargin`) and walk back, as with zone roaming before.
- **The posture holds through a target lapse (item 11, `ScPosture.Update`).** A fight posture whose `ScTeam.Target` is
  null for under `TargetLapseSeconds` (1 s) while its last target lives stays as it is (`posture-lapse-held`).
- **EnemyNotNear halved (D6).** 0-SCore 3.4.25's `IsEnemyNearby` builds its search box at twice the distance (it used the
  distance as the box's full size before), so every `EnemyNotNear` in `Config/utilityai.xml` is halved (30 to 15, 20 to
  10) and `ScSocial.EnemyInSight` asks 15 (`EnemyNearbyDistance`); SC's own `LiveFoe` ranges are unchanged.
- New `ExpectedTargets`: `SdcsMagStageForWrite`, `SdcsMagHandOffForUnload`, `SdcsMagSaveHeld`, and (with Voice Control)
  `VCCommandRouter::MutateSetWeaponPref` and `MutateStealthKill`.

## The NPC's line and voiced dialogue (the voice test, 2026-10-16)

- **The line.** Vanilla 3.2's dialog window shows only your responses, and SCore shows an NPC's line as a subtitle only
  for its older body type, so the player-body survivors' lines never reached the screen. The NPC's current line now
  shows in a panel above the responses, for every NPC but SCore's older type (`DialogLine`; `windowScDialogLine` in the
  vanilla dialog group, `XUiC_ScDialogLine`).
- **Long talk pages (round 28, `DialogPager`).** A talk page with more rows than the dialog window shows (10) splits
  into pages instead of losing its last rows: the mouse wheel over the rows, the gamepad's right stick or the arrows
  beside the "1/2" at the window's top right switch pages, and "Nevermind." is the last row of every page. Any dialog,
  not only the survivors'; a page that fits is unchanged (`ScDialogPager`, a transpiler on the game's response list).
- **The talk page on game 3.4 (round 28, 0-XNPCCore 3.4.04).** Talking to a resident at home (both of you inside the base,
  she not on watch) links her to you while the talk, her craft window, her inventory or her hold-E ring is open
  (`TalkHold`), so 0-XNPCCore's own Craft..., Jobs and gear and Show me your inventory work for her; a craft order or a job
  makes it a work shift, otherwise the link ends 2 s after the last of those windows closed. "Come here" and "Order
  wheel" are a follower's; "Ask to leave" is the last row before "Nevermind." for everyone; no supply run is offered to a
  follower. Members and stand-ins never offer Trade (the radial or by voice) and are never picked up as an item
  (0-XNPCCore 3.4.04's "Pick me up" on the hold-E ring).
- **Voices.** The community's survivors (recruits, radio contacts, members, stand-ins) speak a line when the mod has a
  recording of it: `Resources/Voices/<male|female>_<Race>_<Name>/<line>.ogg`, mono Ogg Vorbis (`VoiceLines`, `ScVoice`).
  - **By race and sex (V2, `VoiceByRace`).** The voices are a table of sixteen, two for each race (White, Black, Asian,
    Native) and sex (`Voices` in `Config/blocks.xml`). Each survivor - and each raider, for its taunts - speaks in one
    of the two voices of their race and sex, read from their body (SC's look), picked from their name, so they keep it
    while the table stays the same. While that voice has no recording of a line, the other voice of their race and sex
    says it, then any recorded voice of their sex, always the same one for that person: everyone keeps talking while a
    race's recordings are still to come. `VoiceByRace` false deals among every recorded voice of the sex, as before.
  - A clip is named after the line's statement id (`scFavourPoi.ogg`), or `<dialog>.<statement>` for another mod's line
    (`SdcsNPC.start.ogg`, 0-XNPCCore's greeting). An enclave request's words, picked at runtime by kind and standing,
    are named after their Localization key (`scEncAsk_gun_friendly.ogg`). A line with a name or a number in it says
    only the part without them; the panel shows the whole line. A `.txt` beside each clip holds the words it says.
  - `python -I Tools/voice_lines.py list | check | cost | sidecars | gencs` lists the voiced lines (`--new` only V2's,
    `--family greet,taunt` one family at a time, `--voice <folder>` only what that voice still lacks, `--tsv` for a
    script), checks every voice of the table and every folder family by family, counts the characters to record per
    voice, writes the `.txt` files, and writes `ScVoiceLines.cs` (V2's lines for the game) from its list.
- **Playback** goes through Voice Control's player on the NPC when Voice Control has set one up, as it does for
  survivors near you: its face lip sync, and its TTS volume and range from its `modconfig.xml`. Otherwise SC plays the
  clip itself, with no lip sync. A new line replaces whatever the NPC was saying, Voice Control's own speech included;
  closing the talk stops only SC's clip. The same line from the same survivor is not spoken again within 30 seconds (it
  still shows), so going back to a talk's first menu doesn't repeat the greeting. SC loads its clips itself and keeps
  at most `VoiceClipCache` (48) of them, the least recently used dropped first (compressed for its own source,
  decompressed for Voice Control's lip sync, which reads the samples); Voice Control never keeps them. Round m1p: and at
  most `VoiceClipCacheMB` (24) megabytes of them (16-bit samples for a decompressed clip, the .ogg's size for a
  compressed one), and a clip nobody asked for in `VoiceClipIdleMinutes` (10) is dropped by a sweep every 30 s. A clip
  is never dropped while it plays or in the 3 s after it was handed out. With 604 lines in 16 voices (2.5 s on average,
  9.7 s at most), 48 decompressed clips are about 10 MB, 34 MB at worst.
- **The shipped voices** are the four White ones, `male_White_Vane`, `male_White_David`, `female_White_Jessica` and
  `female_White_Beth`: each says 147 lines, every survivor line in SC's talks up to E6, the enclaves' included, and
  0-XNPCCore's greeting. They are Vane, David, Jessica and Beth from the ElevenLabs Voice Library, generated with
  ElevenLabs (eleven_v4), one take each - Vane's and Jessica's first seven lines on 2026-10-16, the rest on 2026-10-17
  (round vo2) - and converted to mono Ogg Vorbis. Oxley and Mora are retired (V2). The rest of the table has no
  recordings yet: Black `male_Black_Nate`, `male_Black_Desmond`, `female_Black_Tiffany`, `female_Black_Rene`; Asian
  `male_Asian_Jasper`, `male_Asian_Julian`, `female_Asian_Layla`, `female_Asian_Jess` (the "Jessica NYC" voice);
  Native four voices still to be designed (`male_Native_NativeM1`, `...M2`, `female_Native_NativeF1`, `...F2` until
  they have names - rename them in `Voices`). A new voice is a new folder with the same file names;
  `Tools/voice_lines.py check` shows what it still lacks and `cost` what it costs to record.
- **Closing the talk** stops SC's clip, except an enclave leader's answer to "Let me think about it.": it comes as a
  tell as the talk closes, and is spoken then.
- **Voice Control keeps quiet during the talk (V1, `MuteVcInTalk`).** While you talk to one of the community's survivors,
  and while SC's line still plays on them, 0-XNPCCore's Voice Control does not speak on that survivor: no walk-up or
  follower greeting, say-line ("I'm out of ammo.") or order ack lands over SC's line, nothing it had queued before the
  talk plays over it (a queued sentence its own watchdog releases after the talk has closed can still play then, after
  SC's line), and its HUD subtitle is hidden as the talk opens (a sentence already playing then is cut by SC's line,
  or ends by itself when that line has no recording). Voice Control's calls still finish as if spoken, so it greets and
  chats normally once the talk is over, and SC never flushes its speech queue (that could block every NPC's greeting for
  the session). Speak to the survivor through Voice Control during the talk (push-to-talk, typed chat, or "What are you
  carrying?" on 3.4) and they answer through it until SC's next recorded line. Other NPCs, and SC's survivors outside a
  talk, are untouched. In multiplayer each machine holds its own talk: a line relayed from another player's machine is
  not played on yours while you talk to that survivor. `sc voice gate` shows whom Voice Control is held on and how many
  of its lines were kept quiet; `sc voice sayline [line] [seconds]` gives the nearest survivor one of 0-XNPCCore's
  say-lines through Voice Control after a delay (`ammo.dry`, 5 s), to try it with a talk open.
- **Voice Control's chat knows the enclave (E8, `VoiceEnclaveContext`).** Talk to one of an enclave's people through Voice
  Control's LLM (push-to-talk or `@` chat) and they know their group: its name, how many they are and where they live,
  whether they lead it and who does, how the group stands with you, its open request and by when, an ally's benefit, and
  for one enlisted with you, when they go home. They talk about it but strike no deals by chat: for trading, requests or
  joining they send you to the talk menu. The people of a group you turned hostile refuse to talk, and they and survivors
  with a grudge refuse Voice Control's spoken "trade" too (raiders never chat: Voice Control's `no_voice`). Leaders are told to speak as
  leaders, though Voice Control may still give one a young or nervous voice. Single player only; other NPCs are untouched.
  `sc voice context [id|name]` prints what one of them is told.
- **SC's greetings (V2, `ScGreetings`).** The community's survivors greet you in their own recorded voice, with a line
  that fits who they are to you: a stranger ("Easy now. I'm not looking for trouble."), a recruit, a member at home
  ("Welcome home, boss."), a follower, one of an enclave's people. A survivor greets you the first time each in-game
  day you see them within 12 m (`ScGreetMeetRange`), and when you walk up within 3 m and look at them, at most every
  two minutes each (`ScGreetWalkUpRange`, `ScGreetWalkUpSeconds`); never in a fight or a talk, one at a time
  (`ScGreetGapSeconds`); they turn to you and the words show in Voice Control's subtitle bar. Only where their voice
  has a recording of that greeting - and then Voice Control's own walk-up greeting is skipped for them. Without a
  recording, Voice Control greets them exactly as before. Nobody hostile to you greets, nor an outpost stand-in or a
  runner.
- **Chatter (V2, `ScChatter`).** Every minute and a half or so (`ScChatterSeconds`, `ScChatterChance`), one resident at
  home within 15 m of you says something to nobody in particular ("Somebody check the traps."): recorded lines only,
  one speaker at a time.
- **0-XNPCCore's lines in SC's voices (V2, `ScBarks`).** Where Voice Control is about to speak one of 0-XNPCCore's job
  lines ("I'm out of ammo."), an order reply ("Right behind you."; one generic set of replies for every weapon swap and
  every formation instead of one per weapon or side) or one of its own fixed lines ("I don't take orders from just
  anyone. Hire me first."), and SC has that line recorded in the survivor's voice, SC's recording plays instead,
  through Voice Control's player (lip sync). Without a recording, or while Voice Control is already saying something
  on them, Voice Control speaks as before. Its AI's free speech stays its text-to-speech. Billing lines are not voiced.
- **Raider taunts (V2, `RaiderTaunts`).** Raiders call out when they spot you ("Fresh meat!"), go to look at a noise,
  lose you, are hit, kill you, die, and once when badly hurt ("Fall back!"), and now and then mutter when nobody is
  about. SC plays them from the raider itself (no lip sync), recorded lines only, within 40 m of you
  (`RaiderTauntRange`); one raider at most every 15 s and any two taunts 3 s apart (`RaiderTauntCooldown`,
  `RaiderTauntGlobalCooldown`), so a band doesn't all shout at once.
- **Test aid (`VoiceBarkDebug`).** With it on, a greeting, chatter line or taunt that has no recording yet still shows
  its words and logs `voice bark (silent, no recording): ...`, so the triggers can be watched before the recordings
  exist (Voice Control still greets where SC has no recording).
- **Console:** `sc voice` (the switches, the player in use, the voices, the clip cache, the voice gate),
  `sc voice play <line> [id|name]`, `sc voice stop`, `sc voice rescan`, which picks up new or replaced clips without a
  restart, `sc voice gate` and `sc voice sayline [line] [seconds]`. V2: `sc voice who [id|name]` (race and sex, the
  chosen voice and its fallbacks, which greetings, taunts and XNPCCore lines they can say), `sc voice greet [id|name]`,
  `sc voice chatter [id|name]`, `sc voice taunt <spot|search|lost|hit|killed|idle|dying|retreat> [id|name]`,
  `sc voice barks` (the switches and this session's counts) and `sc voice line <words>` (which of SC's lines Voice
  Control's words are, and whether the nearest survivor has it recorded).

## With SurvivorOrigins: the Leadership skill

The two mods talk only through CVars on the local player; without SurvivorOrigins nothing changes.
- **Published** every second: `sc_followers` (members following you), `sc_inzone` (1 inside the claim),
  `sc_morale_high` (the high-morale threshold), plus `sc_morale` and the `buffSCMorale` HUD buff as before.
- **Counted** (+1, SurvivorOrigins pays XP and resets them): `sc_ev_favour` when a recruit favor is done,
  `sc_ev_rescue` when a runner in distress is rescued, `sc_ev_outpost_retaken` when a contested outpost is cleared
  again (only on a building with sleeper volumes; paid by SurvivorOrigins c8c9266 and later).
- **Applied**: `$soLeadXpMult` multiplies every member's skill and specialty XP; `$soLeadMorale` is a
  "Leadership +N" morale part; `$soLeadShield` shrinks every negative morale part by N, never past 0.

## UI mods (rounds ui1, ui2)

SC's XUi carries a branch per supported UI mod; the analyses behind them (XML replays of every SC patch with and without
each mod, and what each mod's DLL does to the HUD, tab bar and window groups) are in `Port33\research\uicompat\`, with
the replay harness `tools\replay_ui.py` (`SC_PATH=<tree> python -I replay_ui.py base,steelshot <outdir>`).

- **Load order** is the folder names through `Array.Sort` (culture compare: `__` first, case and `-` ignored).
  `SteelshotUi` loads before SC, so SC's patches see its layout; `ZZZ_CATUI` and `ZZZxSteel-UI` load after SC.
- **mod_loaded names:** `CATUI`, `SteelshotUi`, `SteelUI` (`iiMothMan_MothUI` and `PhysicalPDA` for the others). In a
  look conditional the Ashfall UI branch comes first (the first true `<if>` wins), so with Ashfall UI and one of these the
  looks are Ashfall UI's - but the separate position and depth blocks still apply (CATUI's Home Status move and slot lock,
  Steelshot's depth 40), and SteelUI with Ashfall UI loses SC's tabs (SteelUI deletes them and the companion folder skips
  that pair). None of these pairs is supported.
- **Steelshot UI** re-lays `windowPagingHeader` every frame, so its tabs anchor to `#cam`; its backpack backdrop needs
  depth 40 on `windowScMemberInv` / `windowScLinkedStorage`.
- **SteelUI** removes `windowPagingHeader` and builds its own after SC, so SC's header branch for it is empty and the tabs
  come from `Compat/zzzz_SteelUI_SurvivorCommunity` (an XML-only mod folder that sorts after `ZZZxSteel-UI`).
  `Port33\tools\deploy_sc34.py` installs every `Compat/<name>` with a ModInfo.xml as its own `Mods\<name>`; a release
  zip carries it as a third top-level folder next to `SurvivorCommunity` and `LandClaimUpgrade` - build the zip from the
  repo, not from the installed folder, which also holds an inert copy under `SurvivorCommunity\Compat`. SteelUI's fixed 75x75
  `item_stack` is why the member inventory's SteelUI branch uses SC's own `sc_item_stack`
  (`Config/XUi_InGame/templates.xml`, vanilla 3.4's slot with its frame sprites passed as parameters so SteelUI's
  template restyle, keyed on the sprite names, leaves them). `Tools/vanilla_look.conditional(..., extra=[(cond, lines)])`
  emits such extra branches.
- **CATUI** and the Home Status position branches only touch SC's own windows, so they need nothing after the UI mod.
- **Steelshot's tabs** (round ui2) are 30 tall, in the gap between its glass bar (the fill ends 54 down) and vanilla's
  party list (`windowGroupBars`, from 88 down; Steelshot leaves it there).
- **MothUI** (round ui2; `mothui.md`, replay `research\uicompat\tools\mothui\replay_sc_mothui.py <moth|vanilla|ash>
  <outdir>` with `SC_ROOT=<tree>`) loads before SC, so SC patches its replacement windows; its C# (PageBar, MenuLayout,
  HudLayout, ModCompat) runs after. Branch order in each look conditional: Ashfall UI, MothUI, vanilla; a block with no
  Ashfall UI branch guards MothUI's with `and not mod_loaded('zzzzz_AshfallUI')`.
  - **Tabs:** PageBar re-centers every visible tab button by the x it first saw (window space; its bar is 6000 wide from
    -3000), so SC's rects sit at 0,0 and the buttons at 3270 / 3330, after Creative's 3210. Page titles are
    Localization's `mothuiPage<button id>` (`mothuiPageScCommunity`, `mothuiPageScRadio`).
  - **Home Status** carries `mothui_widget` / `mothui_id` (MothUI's ModWidgets; inert without it). The sprite names
    `mui_glass`, `mui_rim` and `mui_accent` are what MothUI's Theme tints, so the renames come after every setter that
    still uses the old names.
  - **Dialog:** MothUI writes the NPC's line into its own card row (`MothUI.XUiC_DialogStatement`, binding
    `mui_statement`, re-bound only when dirty). `ScMothUI.Patch_MothStatement` (found by reflection; `Prepare` is false
    and `ExpectedTargets` skips it without MothUI) swaps in SC's filled line from `XUiC_ScDialogLine.TryFilled`, kept
    whatever `DialogLine` is set to; `XUiC_ScDialogLine` marks that row dirty after each fill. With `DialogLine` and
    `VoiceLines` both off SC reads no line, so MothUI shows its own text, tokens and all. The XML hides SC's own line box
    and pages replies by 6.
  - **Pages:** `ScMothUI.FitPage(page, width, height, frame)` scales `XUiC_ScCommunity` / `XUiC_ScRadio` to fit MothUI's
    frame (216 tall, 80 wide, its MenuLayout's numbers; never below half size) and centers it, or puts it back at its XML
    place and size while the frame (the `windowpaging` group) is closed. Every `XUiV_Window.updateData` runs
    `setRootNode`, which sets `localScale` back to one, so the pages call it after `base.Update` each frame (and from
    OnOpen for the first frame); it writes only what differs. It does nothing unless `ScMothUI.Layout` (MothUI loaded,
    AshfallUI not). A new full-screen page needs the same two calls with its size. `XUiView.GetXUiRect` ignores the
    window's scale (combobox click-to-value, the color picker), so a combobox, slider or color picker on these pages
    would need handling first.
  - **Strips:** `Tools/generate_strips_ui.py`'s `moth` look puts each strip 534 down (8 under MothUI's 526-tall loot
    card) at MothUI's own width binding, `{% v2i_x(gridDimensions) * 70 + 38 }`; the name resolves up the controller
    chain to `XUiC_LootWindow`. `mui_box` takes width and height, not anchors. Known limit: MothUI's MenuLayout scales
    the loot screen without counting the strip, so on an ultrawide screen at a small UI scale (an XUi height above about
    1218 at 21:9) the packing strip's bottom can run past the screen edge.
  - **Not done:** the cosmetic pass (`mothui.md` 6.7) - SC's own windows keep the vanilla look on MothUI's glass.
- **PhysicalPDA** ships its own SC adapter (reflection on SC's types); the user leaves it to its author. Renaming public
  SC members it reads (`ScMorale`, `XUiC_ScCommunity`, `XUiC_ScDialogLine`, ...) breaks it - see the analysis.

## Multiplayer (round MP0 onward)

The multiplayer port runs in two stages:
1. **Stage 1:** a listen host or a dedicated server, one shared community.
2. **Stage 2:** several communities, chosen by a server setting (shared, per player, per party).

Round MP0 is the groundwork:
- **The server is the authority.** The registry lives on the server (single player, listen host or dedicated) and is never
  loaded on a client. `ScRegistry.Loaded` is true only on the server, and a lot of code relies on that, so a client-side
  copy needs its own flag (round MP1: `Mirrored`, below).
- **`ScPlayers`** is the one place SC asks who is playing and where:
  - presence: `AnyHome`, `Home`, `Nearest`, `AnyWithin`;
  - identity: `IdOf`, the platform id `ClientInfo.InternalId` (the host's own is
    `PlatformManager.InternalLocalUserIdentifier`), the key the game itself uses for player files;
  - the community: `Community`, `TellCommunity`.
  - In single player `World.Players.list` holds the one local player, so every "any player" question answers as the old
    "the player" did.
- **The ticker** passes `ScPlayers.Anchor()` down: the host's own player whenever there is one, else the community's owner,
  else a community player at home, else any player. With nobody online it does nothing: an empty dedicated server's
  world time stands still. Tells use the right recipient: the player involved, or the whole community.
- **Records:** members record `leader=` (their leader's platform id) and the community `ownerid=`. A follower whose leader
  link is lost after a relog is restored to their own leader once they are online, never to whoever hosts (single
  player: always the one player).
- **`ScNet`:**
  - `NetPackageScHello` carries SC's protocol number: the game checks package names only, not layouts. Its layout is
    frozen.
  - A client never queues a package the server has no id for (`ServerKnows<T>`): that would disconnect it.
  - `ToPlayer` reaches one player from the server: the local handler for the host's own player, which no package
    reaches; the package for a remote one.
- **Still the host's own until later rounds:**
  - the community window's data and buttons, the land claim's SC entries, SC dialog rows, `sc` console commands (MP1, MP2);
  - linked storage and item hand-overs (MP3);
  - markers, quests, SC's voices and radio lines (MP4);
  - the radio window, enclave dialogs, physical runners, runner distress calls (MP5).
- **Round MP0.1, the "settled" test** (`ScRunners.SettledWhy`, `sc settled`): a server builds a collision-only chunk's
  colliders only around entities (`ChunkManager.GenerateCollidersOnlyAroundEntites`), and frees an empty chunk's. So near a
  player on another machine the test needs the chunks loaded, decorated, lit and not brand new, but no colliders (a body
  spawned there brings them). Near the host's own player it is unchanged.
- **Round MP1, looks (`ScLooks`, "one look on every machine"):** every machine builds SC's bodies (X1), but only the
  server knows their looks. The server is the authority: it never rolls a look because a client asked. It sends a body's
  look (`NetPackageScLook`: `Look.Encode`, `EncodeGear`, the server's source) to a client right before it starts sending
  the body (a prefix on `NetEntityDistributionEntry.updatePlayerEntity`, vanilla's own range test). It has to go before:
  the packages that follow the spawn build the copy at once, since `World.GetEntity` forces a pending body. A copy built
  without it, for example while joining before the hellos are through, wears a placeholder (this machine's earlier look
  for the id, else a roll). It asks with `NetPackageScLookRequest`, once per copy and retried while unanswered, and is
  rebuilt when the answer comes, one body per frame. Every look change on a live body ends in `ScLooks.Rebuild`, which
  sends the new look to the clients that track the body when it differs from what they were sent. A client applies a look
  with raw slot writes: `SetSlotItem`'s equip events would send a buff on a client copy to the server's real body. The
  client keeps the looks for the session, cleared at the world's start and end. X17 never gives a client's copy a creator
  face; the server's arrives with its look.
- **Dedicated-server specifics:**
  - airdrops follow vanilla's schedule (`RadioOnlyAirdrops` applies only with a local player; round MP5 part R: it applies
    while any community player online is not known to run another SC version, below);
  - a client's supply runs are physical and runners call for help there too (round MP5 part N; until then they were hidden
    runs with no distress calls).
- **Round MP1, the mirror** (`ScMirror`, `NetPackageScState`): a client holds a read-only copy of the server's registry in
  `ScRegistry`'s own containers. The community window, the Home Status HUD, the Facilities tab, the talk card and the
  helpers under them read it unchanged.
  - **The text:** `ScRegistry.BuildText(forClients)` is the save file's text; `ParseText` reads both. For clients it leaves
    out:
    - platform ids, the cleanup bookkeeping and streamer names;
    - members' entity snapshots (`snap=` and `postsnap=` become `-`, which keeps their meaning: the member is held
      offline);
    - handed items, worn pieces and ammo;
    - enclave trade stock, and the enclaves' and bands' people.
  - **The flags:** `ScRegistry.Loaded` still means "the authoritative registry" (the server only), and code that writes
    keeps asking it or `IsHost`. `Mirrored` is a client holding a copy; `Ready` (either one) is for read gates only.
    `ScMirror.Active` is a client with a copy.
  - **The push:** a client that said hello with SC's protocol gets the whole snapshot at once. After that the ticker sends,
    at most once a second, the snapshot if `SaveAtomic` ran since, or else the small derived block if it changed (checked
    every 2 s). The host's own player and single player get nothing.
  - **The derived block:** what a client cannot work out itself. It holds morale and its parts, members' moods, attention,
    the activities' countdowns, radio online, the defense rating and horde, the next rollover's needs and daily flow,
    noise, beds with the allies', outpost slots, the bundle share, contested outposts' remaining sleepers, and per member
    the missing set, health, downed and fragile time, and the held order. While `ScMirror.Active`, the helpers answer from
    it (`ScMorale.Compute`, `ScStockpile.NeedForRollover`, `ScEconomy.DailyFlow`, `ScOutposts.RadioOnlineAnywhere`,
    `ScMissing.IsMissing`, `ScDowned.StatusText` ...). A few live figures are written into the copy.
  - **The rule for new code:** a path that runs on a client (patches on entities, render, avatar or UI; dialogs; block
    commands) and acts on a registry record must check `ScRegistry.Loaded` or `ScCommunity.IsHost`. The window's buttons
    say scHostOnly on a client until round MP2 adds requests.
- **Round MP2, the community window, its Facilities tab and the radials (`ScActionsWindow`):** every button that changes
  the community calls `ScActions.Request` on every machine, the host included, and shows the answer's text and sound where
  the old inline code showed its result. The handlers run on the server with the player who asked and re-check everything
  with the verbs' own tests.
  - **Ops** (whole numbers unless marked s:):
    - `member.run` (member, kind, job): a supply run (kind = `Resource`, job -1) or a mining run (kind -1, job = `RunJob`);
    - `member.work` (member, intent: 1 Call to work, 0 End work): Call to work / End work;
    - `member.workend` (member): "That's all for now.", silent when she is on no shift;
    - `member.role` (member, role 0-3, want: 1 on, 0 off): a crafting role on or off;
    - `member.post` (member, outpost id): answers the hint `outpost=<id>`;
    - `member.watch` (member, x, y, z): the guard for that Watch post;
    - `member.recallposted` (member): a staff row's Recall;
    - `outpost.disband` (outpost id, forget 0/1), Owner access: disband a held outpost, or forget a lost one's record;
    - `outpost.upgrade` (outpost id, seen: 0 Repair, else the level the Upgrade was pressed at): repairs a damaged
      outpost, else buys its next level;
    - `outpost.mode` (outpost id, seen: the mode shown, 0-2);
    - `facility.do` (slot, member or -1, pick state or -1; s: entry key, s: action id): one action of the Facilities tab;
    - `facility.upgrade` (x, y, z, seen: the level shown): the well or dew collector radial's Upgrade;
    - `watch.relieve` (x, y, z): the Watch post radial's Relieve guard.
  - **Intent (the review's M1):** a toggle or a two-purpose button sends what it meant as its LAST int (absent or -1: no
    check). When the server's state no longer matches it answers `scActionChanged` instead of doing the opposite (a second
    Repair buying a level, a second Call to work ending the shift). The window computes the intent from the same state the
    handler reads (`ScActionsWindow.UpgradeIntent`, `ModeIntent`, `ScWork.OnWorkShiftView`), so on the host it always
    matches. A new toggle op should do the same.
  - **The Facilities tab:** `ScFacilityTab.Build(viewer)` builds the entries for a player (a provider is
    `(List<FacilityEntry> into, EntityPlayer viewer)`), so a client builds its tab from its copy with its own player and the
    server rebuilds the entry for the player who pressed. `FacilityAction.Run` and `Pick` take an `EntityPlayer`. Give every
    action an `Id` (its label's localization key): `facility.do` matches a pressed button by its slot and that id, so a
    list that changed in between, or a client in another language, cannot fire the wrong action. An action whose meaning
    depends on state puts that state in its id (`scUiFacUpgrade@<level>`, `scUiFacAssignGuard@x,y,z`), so the match
    refuses a stale press; a pick that does one of two things by the member's state sets `PickState` (Call to work: 1 call,
    0 end), which the client sends with the pick. An action that needs more than Community access sets `Access`
    (rationing: `Owner`). `Ready` and `WhyNot` on a client are hints from its copy.
  - **Reads and writes:** the checks the window, the tab and the radials read for display (`ScRuns.CanSend`,
    `ScWork.CanStart`, `ScOutpostPerks.CanUpgrade`, `ScActivities.WhyNot*`, `ScLightsOut.WhyNot`) answer from a client's copy
    (`ScRegistry.Ready`). The verbs that write (`Send`, `Start`, `CallOrEnd`, `Upgrade`, `Repair`, `PourDrinks`,
    `PrepareFeast`, `EnactRationing`, `Order`, `TryUpgrade`, `Assign`, `Relieve` ...) refuse unless `ScRegistry.Loaded`.
    `ScActionsWindow.CanRequest` says whether this machine can ask the server at all (the radials show their SC commands
    by it).
  - **`sc_shift`** (a member on a work shift) is sent to the clients and set to 0 when the shift ends, no longer removed:
    the dialog rows read it there, and a removal never reaches a client. Test it with `> 0`. The shift's `Owner` link is
    force-sent too, and `ScCommunity.Detach` clears `Owner` with `ClearSynced` (clients get a 0; the server removes it as
    before). SCore and 0-XNPCCore treat `Owner` 0 as no owner (their own Dismiss writes 0).

- **Round MP2, the land claim radial and the console** (`ScActionsClaim`, `TEFeatureSCCommunity`, `ConsoleCmdSc`,
  `ConsoleCmdScl`):
  - **Ops** (ScActions requests; the radial asks on every machine, the handler runs on the server):
    - `home.set x y z [mode]` (Owner): the claim becomes the home and the SENDER its owner (`ScZone.SetHome`: platform
      id). `mode` is an optional fourth int, 0 when absent: 0 sets or moves the home; 1 is Start a community here, refused
      (`scHomeAlreadyStarted`) once a home exists, so two players starting at once get one community; 2 and 3 are
      AutoHomeFirstClaim's silent adoption by Community / Linked storage, which do nothing once a home exists.
    - `outpost.establish x y z` (Community): `ScOutposts.Establish` with every gate of `CanEstablish`.
    - `outpost.disbandclaim x y z` (Owner): the outpost on that claim, `ScOutposts.Disband` (which tells the sender).
    - Every handler first checks the claim against the sender in the server's claim map
      (`ScActionsClaim.WhyNotOwnClaim`: an active land claim of theirs). The radial's `LocalPlayerIsOwner` only runs on
      the pressing machine, for its own player.
  - **The radial** shows on a client from its copy (`ScRegistry.Ready`). The two-press Disband confirm is the pressing
    machine's own state (`ScOutposts.DisbandPressed`); the second press sends the request. Linked storage and the outpost's
    home link still refuse on a client (scHostOnly) until MP3. An Outpost Claim Block queues itself on the server when any
    online community player placed it (`ScActionsClaim.PlacerOf`: the composite's owner, set from the placer).
  - **Markers:** an outpost a remote player establishes is marked on that player's machine only from MP4 on;
    `ScOutposts.HostMarkersFollow` keeps the listen host's own map up to date meanwhile.
  - **`sc`** is no client command, so a client's `sc` runs on the server. It acts for the player who typed it:
    `ConsoleCmdSc.Me`, the client's entity from `CommandSenderInfo.RemoteClientInfo`, else the local player, else null
    (telnet, the web console, a dedicated server's own console: the old "no player" answers). Code in `ConsoleCmdSc` uses
    `Me`, never `ScCommunity.LocalPlayer`. `sc lightsout on|off` acts for `Me` too. Not for a client until a later round
    (MP5: the radio and the world): `sc barter`, `sc scout`, `sc infest add` and `sc outpost pay|refuse` pass
    `Me as EntityPlayerLocal` (no player for a client), `sc enclave refill now` and the enclave and band commands' own
    handlers read the host's own player.
  - **`scl`** (`ConsoleCmdScl`, long name `survivorcommunitylocal`) is a client command (`IsExecuteOnClient`, permission
    level 1000: any player). It runs `ui [reset]`, `portrait ...` and `voice [play|stop|rescan]` in the typing player's own
    game through the same code (`ConsoleCmdSc.ExecuteClientLocal`), reading only that machine's copy. The game runs a
    command on the client as a whole, never per subcommand, hence two commands. A client's `sc ui`, `sc portrait` and
    `sc voice play|stop|rescan` answer "type scl ... instead".

- **Round MP2 (part M), member orders and the talk** (`ScActionsMembers`, `ScTalkContext`, `NetPackageScTalkContext`):
  - **Ops** (`[ScAction]` handlers; ints, `member` = `Member.Id`, `npc` = entity id): `member.follow` (member, mode: 0 or
    absent the window's branch - call back a missing member (hint `select` = the new id), turn round one on the way to an
    outpost, recall a runner (the radio rule), recall a posted one, send a follower home, else follow; 1 the talk's "Follow
    me"; 2 the talk's "Head home" to a runner met outside); `member.home` (member, mode 1: the talk's); `member.leave`
    (member, mode; Owner access; mode 0 refuses a downed member with scDownedBusy); `member.join` (npc, journal: 1 when the
    asking player's POI favour quest is past its clearing); `favour.start` (npc, kind = `FavourKind`); `favour.poicleared`
    (npc, journal); `favour.items` (npc, any) and `favour.given` (npc, any, resource, taken): the supplies hand-over;
    `band.join` (npc). A remote player's favour quest closes on their own machine: the reply's hint `fclose` (prospect id)
    and `fdone` (1 completed, 0 removed), done by `ScTalkContext.ApplyQuestHint`.
  - **The talk context:** a client sends `Open` (npc, its journal's verdict) when a talk with an SCore NPC opens (a postfix on
    `XUiC_DialogWindowGroup.OnOpen`); the server rolls the profile and the favour there (never on a client: E4) and answers
    with `ScTalkFacts` (the recruit verdict and reason, the home, the favour, the POI's sleeper verdict, a band's answers and
    line tokens). The client keeps it per NPC, re-evaluates the open response list, and picks the first statement again
    while the talk still shows the one it opened on (a band's greeting is a phase). Every SC dialog requirement keeps its
    code on the server and has a client path that reads the facts, the copy (`ScTalkContext.View` builds a NEW prospect -
    the copy is never changed) and the client's own journal and inventory. A new requirement that needs server-only state
    adds a field to `ScTalkFacts` (and bumps `ScNet.Protocol`).
  - **Order on the wire:** a client's "Mark it" sends `favour.start` BEFORE the quest goes into its journal: the reliable
    channel keeps order, so the server holds the POI as a favour's before the sleeper subscribe `AddQuest` sends arrives
    (E6: `ScQuests.IsOurPoi(pos, subscribers)` answers from the registry - POI favours taken on and not done, accepted
    request places - whenever a remote player subscribed). The host keeps its old order (quest first, then the mark).
  - **The supplies hand-over** is two steps: the server says what to take (or refuses: no home, roster full), the client
    takes it from its own bag recording what left, the server checks again and marks the favour done - or refuses with
    `refund=1` and the client gives the items back. The row's `ScJoin` waits for the hand-over on a client.
  - **A favour is shared** (two players may take the same one): whoever finishes it finishes it for everyone; when they
    join, die or turn hostile every other player's copy of the quest is removed (`ScTalkContext.CloseFavourForOthers`,
    the package's `Close`).
  - **Cvars the talk's rows read** (`sc_member`, `sc_following`, `sc_onrun`, `sc_favour`, `sc_favourDone`): write them with
    `ScCommunity.SetSynced` (force-sent, only on a change) and clear them with `ScCommunity.ClearSynced` (clients get a 0,
    then the server removes it, so every server-side reader and the save see what they always did). Code that runs on a
    client tests the value (`> 0`), never `HasCustomVar`.
  - **No home by talking:** for a remote player the talk context, the hand-over, `member.join` and a band's line read
    whether the home stands (`ScZone.EnsureHomeFor`: `HomeStands`), never adopt a claim with AutoHomeFirstClaim; only the
    host's own player keeps `EnsureHome`. A client's home comes from the radial (`home.set`, an Owner op).
  - **In person:** the talk modes of `member.follow` / `member.home`, `member.join`, `band.join` and the `favour.*` ops
    need a remote sender within `ScTalkContext.TalkRange` (20 m) of the live body; out of reach is answered as no body
    (silent; a hand-over's items go back).
  - **A new talk drops the old facts:** `ScTalkContext.OnTalkOpened` forgets the facts of an earlier talk with that NPC
    before it asks again, so the rows wait for this talk's answer.
- **Known gaps after the MP2 review (later rounds):**
  - A supplies hand-over whose answer never comes (a dropped connection) is not refunded, and its row's `ScJoin` waits 30 s
    (members MINOR 2, NIT 2): MP3's item transactions.
  - E6: `Prospect.Started` never goes back when a remote player drops the favour quest, so that POI's vanilla clear
    quests are not re-armed for remote players until the favour is done (members MINOR 7): MP4's journal report. (Closed in
    round MP4, part Q: below.)
  - The window's Home / Leave keep a `done` fallback text that is never used (core n5); one helper for the `sc_shift` 0
    and `ClearSynced` (core n1).
  - No per-player request budget; no range check on the three claim ops (only the sender's own claim passes) (radials NIT 2).
  - A client sees a band line's tokens raw for one round trip; two tooltips when a client's request fails at once; a
    controller's cursor jumps to the first row when facts land (members NIT 3-5).

- **Round MP3 (part T), the packing station and bundle opening** (`ScActionsStock`, `ScStockpile`, `XUiC_ScPackingStrip`):
  an open box belongs to the player who has it open (the server's writes into a box a client has open are thrown away,
  then overwritten by the client's copy), so the item side runs on the machine that has the station open, in its own open
  grid, and the server changes only the stockpile and the XP. The strip calls the ops on every machine; the host's run
  inline, so both halves run back to back there with the old checks, lines and order.
  - **Ops** (whole numbers unless marked s:; x, y, z = the station's block): `stock.packed` (x, y, z, resource, units,
    large, medium, small, threshold) after a pack in the grid: the craftsmen's XP; `stock.deposit` (x, y, z, bundles, then
    the units per `Resource`, 7) after the bundles left the grid: credit (a refusal puts them back: their slots, any empty
    slot, the bag, the feet); `stock.withdraw` (x, y, z, resource, room 1/0) before the bundle goes into the grid: debit,
    with a refund `ticket` hint; `stock.refund` (ticket) when the bundle no longer fit or the box was closed in between (a
    refused refund puts the bundle into the bag instead); `bundle.opened` (s: the bundle's item name): the Logistics XP
    (the open itself stays the opening machine's, as vanilla). Deposit, withdraw and refund answer the `stock` hint (the 7
    figures after the op), which a client's strip shows until its copy changes (`ScMirror.Version`).
  - **Checks:** the position holds a packing station or a held outpost claim's box (`ScStockpile.StationOf`); a remote
    sender is within the loot window's own closing distance of it (plus slack) and holds its open lock (`LockManager`'s
    single lock on that storage feature, channel 0). The amounts are the client's (co-op trust), checked only for
    plausibility. Tickets are single-use, the sender's own, 120 s.
  - **A client's outposts:** its `ScOutposts` flags are never built, so `StationOf` reads the copy's outposts there.
  - **`ScStockpile.GiveBundles`** takes any `EntityPlayer`: this machine's own player gets the bag, then the feet, as
    before; for a remote player on the server pass a `toRemote` list and send it in the reply (`ScItemTx.StacksToHint`),
    for that player's machine to add (`ScItemTx.GiveToLocal`). Without the list the bundles drop at their feet. Never give
    into a box (`st`) this machine does not have open.
  - **Known gaps:** a reply that never comes (a disconnect; `ScActions` keeps a client's callback 900 s, so a slow server's
    late answer still settles) loses what was in flight: a deposit's bundles to the player, a withdrawal's unit to the
    community. A refused `stock.packed` keeps the bundles and pays no XP. A client packs with its copy's Craftsmanship stars,
    which can be one push behind. Co-op trust, left as is (MP3 review, packing/gear m6 and m7): `bundle.opened` and
    `stock.packed` pay their XP with no per-player rate limit, so a modified client could farm Logistics or Craftsmanship XP;
    and a withdrawal's refund ticket stays valid for its 120 s even after the bundle went into the box, so a modified client
    could refund a unit it keeps. Neither adds an item a modified client could not already claim through `stock.deposit`.

- **Round MP3 (part G), the member panel's gear and station crafting** (`ScActionsGear`, `ScMemberEquip`, `ScAmmoChoice`,
  `XUiC_ScMemberInv`, `ScCraftStation`, `Patches_Craft`):
  - **Ops** (ints unless marked s:; `npc` = the member's entity id; `kind` = `ScMemberEquip.GearKind`, 1 weapon, 2 armor,
    3 tool): `member.geardrop` (npc, kind, index; s: the stack, `ScItemTx.Encode`), `member.geartake` (npc, kind, index,
    toBackpack 0/1), `member.ammo` (npc, step 1/-1: `ScAmmoChoice.Cycle`), `member.ammoline` (npc: a read, hints `show`,
    `key`, `ammo`, `n` - the client formats the line in its own language). The panel asks on every machine; the host's own
    request works on its cursor and container in place (no encoding), exactly as before. A remote player's reply carries
    hints `cur` (the stack for their cursor), `give` (stacks for their bag) and `pt` (one item their machine takes out of
    its own copy of her pack), each `ScItemTx.StacksToHint`.
  - **Her pack is the opener's for the session.** SCore sends a remote opener a copy and merges it back on close by what
    they changed (`HarvestManager.EndRemoteSession`): a server-side ADDITION to the pack during the session survives the
    merge, a server-side REMOVAL would not show in their grid and could leave twice. So while a remote player has her
    panel open the server only adds to her pack (a stale stored piece; it shows at the next open), never takes from it:
    the job tool slots on a client move the tool in its own copy (`ScMemberEquip.CopyDropTool` / `CopyTakeTool`, no
    request, exactly a drag in the grid), and the Wardrobe pick's real piece in her pack comes back as `pt` (the client's
    `TakeFromCopy`, matched by SCore's `EntitySyncUtils.ItemKey`). Her bag, her pair, her look and her record are the
    server's alone and change there. The client's pack grid needs no refresh: everything it shows changed in it.
  - **Take first:** a client's dropped stack leaves its cursor when the request goes; a refusal (or a server that cannot
    answer) gives it back, to the cursor while the panel is open and empty, else the bag (`ScItemTx.GiveToLocal`). One
    gear request at a time (10 s); a late answer still settles its own items. A reply's stack too large to encode drops at
    the player's feet on the server.
  - **`ScMemberEquip.Gate`** takes an `EntityPlayer`. The requesting player's own open copy is no obstacle
    (`ScAmmo.IsRemoteOpenByOther`), anyone else's still refuses, and a remote player needs SCore's session of their own
    (`OwnsCopy`; `scInvRefuseReopen` otherwise). With `ScMemberEquip.BeginRemote` set (the handler, around one call) the
    backpack room test and the backpack put are the client's, and `GiveToPlayer` with no local player collects into the
    reply instead of dropping at her feet.
  - **The bag stays the host's** (the panel's note). The game never sends an NPC's bag; it changes on the server with every
    reload, magazine fill and resupply while the panel is open, so a client view would need a server compare per cell:
    risk and size out of proportion to what is in it (rounds and magazines SC manages itself).
  - **Voice Control's ammo pick** already writes the server's record: Voice Control runs `MutateSetAmmo` only on the server
    (`ExecuteAuthoritative`), so `Patch_VcSetAmmo` fires there for every player.
  - **Station crafting (c2 for clients):** a client's station window makes the queue on its own copy of the station (it
    holds the lock; the copy is written back on every change and on close). `ScCraftStation.MemberStack` and `KeepApart`
    read `ScRegistry.Ready` (the client's copy of the registry; `Loaded` on the server, so the host is unchanged): her
    item goes into the station's output, never the client's inventory, listed under her id, with no CraftedItem (and no
    Survivor Origins XP) for the client. C1e never sweeps on a client; the server sweeps instead when a REMOTE player
    releases the station (`Patch_StationSweepRemote`, a postfix on `TileEntity.OnUnlockedServer` for workstations: the
    close's last write-back is in by then, on the same ordered channel). 0-XNPCCore's own pay (C1d) waits while a station is
    locked (`SdcsStations.IsFree`), so nothing is paid twice. The host's own unlock is left to its C1e at the next open.
    MP3 review (packing/gear m2): a FORCED unlock (no keep-open from the client for 10 s) also runs that sweep, and if the
    client's link then recovers, its queued station writes bring the paid entries back (the server reads a client's tile
    entity with no lock test). So for 60 s after a remote sweep, a client write to that station while nobody holds it is
    trimmed (`Patch_StationLateWriteBack`, a prefix and postfix on `TileEntityWorkstation.read` for `FromClient`): per entry
    (crafter, recipe, item, scrapped item), what the write added over the server's list, up to what the sweep paid, is taken
    off again unpaid (`sc craft`: `c1e-late-write-back-trimmed`). A lock holder's own write-back is never touched, and a
    craft the client's window made after the sweep is the excess beyond that and is paid as usual. XP only.
  - **ExpectedTargets:** `TileEntity::OnUnlockedServer` (`Patches_Craft.UnlockedTarget()`) and `TileEntityWorkstation::read`
    (`Patches_Craft.WorkstationReadTarget()`). `ScArms.PanelWeapon` still takes `EntityPlayerLocal` (`ScMemberEquip` passes
    `player as EntityPlayerLocal`; its one use goes through `GiveToPlayer`).
  - **`ScItemTx.GiveToLocal` and AutoEquip armor** (MP3 review, packing/gear m4): `XUiM_PlayerInventory.AddItem` puts an
    `AutoEquip` armor piece ON and then places the piece it took off; with a full bag that old piece is lost and the new one
    reported as left over. `GiveToLocal` therefore puts such a piece into the bag or the toolbelt as an item
    (`AddItemToBackpack`, `AddItemToToolbelt`) and drops it at the player's feet when neither has room; it never wears it.
  - **Known gaps:** a request whose answer never comes (a dropped connection) loses the stack in flight. A client that
    disconnects with her panel open keeps what it took from her pack and loses what it put in (SCore's session ends with
    the server's contents: its own behavior for every grid move). The server trusts the stack a client sends (the item and
    its data, as every MP3 take). A stale piece the server added shows only at the next open. A member's station crafting XP
    can be paid twice in one narrow case: a client's late write-back to a station lands while ANOTHER player already holds it
    open after a forced unlock (the trim of the remote sweep then skips it). XP only - no item is copied or cut.

- **Round mt1 (part G), the member panel shows her real gear and holds her** (`ScMemberGear`, `ScActionsGear`,
  `XUiC_ScMemberInv`, `ScHanded`, `ScAmmoChoice`, `Patches_VoiceControl.Patch_PanelHold`): fixes for the first two-player
  test ("taking/swapping weapons re-rolls the quality/stats", "the equipment menu stays up when the NPC walks away", a
  client's empty job tool slots).
  - **Stats (every mode):** `ScHanded.Imprint` copies 3.4's rolled `ItemValue.Stats` (a deep array copy) besides quality,
    seed, mods and wear, and `SameBuild`, `Fingerprint` and `ItemText` ("stats n") know them. Before, every copy of a handed
    weapon fought without the handed item's rolls. `NeedsRebuild` sees the difference, so each handed weapon's held model is
    rebuilt once after load. The panel's shown hand copy is imprinted the same way (a display clone, never her copy), and
    its last-resort item is the same every refresh (`ScMemberGear.Placeholder`: seed 0), where a new seed each half second
    made the slot's item and its seed-rolled numbers change.
  - **`member.gearview` (npc; a quiet read, Community):** the equipment column as the server sees it (`ScMemberGear.GearView`,
    built by the same `Weapon` / `Armor` / `ServerTools` / `ScAmmoChoice.LineParts` the host's panel calls inline). Hints
    (`ScMemberGear.PutView` / `ReadView`): `v`=1; per weapon `vr*` / `vm*` - `k` kind (0 none, 1 no ranged, 2 item, 3 a pair
    token that is no item), `i` the item name, `s` the stack (`ScItemTx.StacksToHint`), `p` the info line's numbers
    "quality,gun,loaded,capacity,mags,spare,wear%"; `va` the armor state (0 no look, 1 sent, 2 left out) and `va0`..`va3`
    the pieces; `vt<job>` "store,slot" and `vt<job>s` the stack of the server's best tool from her bag and toolbelt;
    `vl` 0/1, `vlk`, `vla`, `vln` the ammo line's key, ammo and count. Every text is made on the client, in its language.
    A remote `member.geardrop` / `geartake` reply carries the same view after its own `cur` / `give` / `pt`. Size: a view
    is a few thousand characters; `PutView` keeps a view under 16 000 and a whole gear reply under 24 000 (what does not fit
    is left out: the tools first, then the armor; `NetPackageScResult` has no cap of its own, and the game's connection takes
    about 2 MB a package).
  - **A client's panel** asks every 1.5 s while open, one request at a time, at once after an ammo click or a gear reply
    without a view, and drops an answer of an earlier opening (`viewToken`). Until the first answer RANGED and MELEE show
    their names only, the armor slots are bare, and the job slots show its own copy of her pack. The job slots merge that
    copy (which the client edits itself, round MP3) with the server's bag and toolbelt picks (`ScMemberGear.Tools(npc,
    window, server)`), so the work kit shows; a slot showing a server pick refuses a take as the host refuses the kit
    (`scInvRefuseKit`, `scInvRefuseKitPair`, `scInvRefuseBagClient`). A client no longer scans its own toolbelt copy (a local
    roll without the kit). `member.ammoline` stays on the server; SC's own panel no longer polls it. The host and single
    player build the column inline, as before.
  - **No by-name re-arm on close (F3):** `XUiC_ScMemberInvGroup.ReArm` skips SCore's `UpdateWeapon()` / `SendSyncData(1)`
    when `CurrentNPC` is a member (it re-equipped her remembered weapon by NAME: a random quality without mods or stats on
    every close). Every panel change already armed her with the exact copy. Another NPC keeps SCore's re-read.
  - **The hold (H1, every mode):** a Harmony postfix on Voice Control's private static
    `NPCChatComponent.LocalHarvestWindowOpenOn(EntityAlive)` (bound by name, ExpectedTargets
    `NPCChatComponent::LocalHarvestWindowOpenOn`, one error line and off when it is missing) answers true while SC's panel
    shows her (`XUiC_ScMemberInvGroup.HoldsNpc`, one compare per call) or Voice Control's own equipment window shows her
    (`XUiC_NpcEquipWindow.s_npcId`, optional). Voice Control's hold only gates `NoActiveChat`: guard, stay, wander, the
    follows, patrols, routine walks, the medic walk, the jobs and SC's post, roam, quiet night, chat and gathering. Fights,
    cover, flee, LeaveArea, SC's base defense and investigate walk are ungated. On a client its own component tells the
    server (`NetPackageVCChatState`: `SetRemoteChatActive` for 45 s, refreshed every 20 s, false on close).
  - **Closing at a person's reach (H3, every mode):** the panel closes when she is more than 6 m away horizontally or 3 m
    vertically for 1 s (the limits follow the nearest she has been since the open, plus 2 m / 1.5 m), with the tell
    `scInvClosedAway` ("{0} had to go."). The hit, downed, death and 32 m closes stay.
  - **Items:** nothing here moves an item. The view is display only, the gear requests are round MP3's, and a late view after
    the close is dropped while a late gear reply still settles its items (`Settle`).
  - **Known gaps:** Voice Control stows her weapon while the panel holds her and for 20 s after (as in any talk). The kit is
    still missing from a client's copy of her body (its 3D hand while she works); only the panel shows the server's.
  - **Review fixes:** while her hand is holstered (`Hand.IsHolstered`: Bare mode) `holdingItemItemValue` is the bare hand, so
    `ScSkills.HeldOrStowed` / `HoldsRangedOrStowed` read the selected slot's item instead (`inventory.GetItem(holdingItemIdx)`):
    `ScLoadout.Set` re-arms the kind she will draw, the panel's "hand" step and `ScArms.NoteOrdered` name that item, and over
    an empty slot nothing is noted - a swap made while she is stowed no longer flips a gun holder to melee or tells
    0-XNPCCore the bare hand was ordered (single player and the host get their pre-mt1 behavior back). `ScPanelHold` (H2,
    the server): from the 1 Hz ticker, for each of SCore's session marks (`ScAmmo.RemoteMarks`) on a member whose remote
    player is alive within 10 m, Voice Control's public `ChatComponentManager.GetOrCreate(npc).SetRemoteChatActive(player,
    true)` (reflection, bound once, one error line and off when missing) at once, 3 s later (the client's own "false" from its
    dialog's close can land after the first) and then every 20 s; `false` on `RemoteClosed`, a stale mark, distance, death
    and disconnect (`sc xnpc` reports it). Voice Control keeps one window per player and NPC, so that `false` also ends the
    client's own window while its dialog with her is open (its keepalive restores it within 20 s). `member.gearview` answers
    only a sender with her inventory open (`ScAmmo.IsRemoteOpenBy`) or within 10 m, else a quiet refusal; the view carries
    `vn` (her entity id, checked by `ReadView`), and `ReadView` splits the hint once into a table. A weapon whose stack was too
    large for the view (its slot shows the name over an empty slot) can be taken: `XUiC_ScGearSlot.HasItem` asks the panel's
    view (`ViewNamesItem`). Voice Control's equipment window NPC is read through a `StaticFieldRefAccess` field ref (no
    boxing per NPC per frame). `scInvRefuseBagClient` no longer names a host's screen. Left as they are: two view polls can be
    in flight for a moment after the 10 s give-up (harmless); until the first view RANGED, MELEE and the armor take nothing
    (one round trip); and single player's display-only changes (the imprinted hand copy, seed-0 last-resort items, the info
    line built from numbers) are intended.

- **Round MP3 (part L), linked storage** (`ScStorageServer`, `NetPackageScStorage`, `ScStorage`, `ScLinkedContainer34`,
  `ScHomeLink`, `Patches_LinkedStorage`): the server owns the boxes; every player's view, the host's own included, goes
  through the same claim and compare-and-set path.
  - **Ops** (ScActions, Community access):
    - `storage.open` (kind, x, y, z): kind `ScStorage.KindHome` 0 (the window's Open storage button, the home claim's
      Linked storage; x y z unused), `KindOutpost` 1 (Outpost storage at that claim), `KindHomeLink` 2 (the home's boxes
      from the outpost claim at x y z: `ScHomeLink` loads the home zone per player and answers "Radioing home...").
      The answer only carries a refusal; the view comes as `NetPackageScStorage` (the host's own player gets it inline).
      Refused (`scStorageTooFar`) when the sender stands more than 40 m (flat) from the view's anchor: the home claim, or
      the claim pressed (MP3 review, storage m4).
    - `storage.cells` (session, batch, part, parts, then per entry flags, x, y, z, index; s: per entry expect and new for
      a compare-and-set, or the stack and "" for a deposit, flags bit 0): one batch of cell changes, in parts of
      `ScStorageServer.PartEntries` (50; a request holds 255 ints), judged whole at the last part. The answer's hints:
      `res` (per entry `x,y,z,index,code`, '=' as '~', `!` = the box is gone or out of reach, `-` = a deposit not placed;
      the slots after the batch, or as they are now when refused), `fs` and `fc` (the view's free slots and free cells,
      `fc` -1 without the grid bridge on the server). A batch that is written but handed a deposit back answers Done with
      `scStorageFull`. Refused with no `res` (`scStorageClosedFar`) more than 40 m from the anchor, except while the player is
      dead or just respawned (their held batch comes from the spawn point).
    - `storage.close` (session), `storage.keep` (session, active; every 5 s while the view is open, and for a home link
      view waiting for another window: active 1 when the window's cursor moved since the last keep).
  - **`NetPackageScStorage`** (server to client; layout in its header): the view (session, kind, anchor, outpost name,
    counts, then every stack as box x y z, slot, code), slot updates (another view wrote boxes this view shows), and "the
    server ended the view" (a reason key).
  - **Sessions and claims:** one session per player holds the boxes its view shows - the boxes it may write, nothing more
    (MP3 review, storage M2: a claim used to keep hand-opens and NPC jobs out of every box of the base, so one AFK player's
    open view blocked the whole base). Every write is judged against the box itself at that moment, so a claim keeps nobody
    out:
    - a hand-open goes ahead, and the box leaves every view that showed it (`ScStorageServer.OnVanillaOpen`, a postfix on
      `TEFeatureStorage.OnLockRequestServer` once the lock is granted): its cells are pushed as gone (`!`), and a view's
      change to it is refused (out of the session, and open by someone). The opener's own (closing) view only loses that box;
    - the NPC writers write it like any other box and push the slots they wrote (`ScStorageServer.PushWritten`); a view's
      change made against the old content is refused by the compare-and-set and resynced.
    Two players' views may claim the same box: their changes are compared against the box itself, and every commit is pushed
    to the other views that show the slot. A session ends on `storage.close`, on a new open, on the player's disconnect, 40 m
    from the view's anchor, after 20 s without `storage.keep` (a remote player only: the host's own requests run inline, so a
    long frame or a suspended PC is no silence; MP3 review, storage m8), after 5 minutes with no cell change and no use of
    the window (`ScStorageServer.IdleTimeout`, `scStorageClosedIdle` told to the player; the host's own view only while
    another player is in the world, never in single player), or at the world's start and end (`ScStorageServer.Sweep`, once
    a second, first thing in `ScTicker`'s tick in its own try: MP3 review, storage m7). A dead player's view is held (claims
    kept, no distance, silence or idle test) for up to 2 minutes and 15 s past the respawn: ScActions refuses a dead player's
    requests, so a batch that reached the server just after the death is held by the view and sent again after the respawn,
    and the view's close waits for the respawn too. While held, its cells count as in flight (no push overwrites them), and
    a refused resend takes back what the batch's own expect codes showed (MP3 review, storage m2). No claim can outlive its
    player.
  - **The F3-F5 rules, for everyone (single player too):** a view leaves out a box that somebody has open
    (`LockManager.IsLockedServer`, or the server's own open flag) and one locked (`TEFeatureLockable`) against the viewer.
    `DepositInZone`, `TopUpInZone` and `TakeOneInZone` skip a box that is open or locked against the community's owner (the
    host's own player while a save has no owner id yet). `ScJobs.CarryWindow` drops what fits neither a re-created member's
    window nor a home crate in one bag at her feet (`ScArms.DropBag`), never deletes it.
  - **Compare-and-set on the encoding:** stacks are compared as `ScItemTx.Encode` strings, never with `ItemValue.Equals`
    (it compares mod arrays by reference, so two copies of one modded gun never match). The view echoes the code the server
    sent, so the server always compares its own encoding with itself. The host's own view never leaves the machine, so its
    codes are not capped at 4000 characters (`ScStorageServer.CodeFor` / `StackOf`: `ScItemTx.Encode` below the cap, the same
    base64 of `ItemStack.Write` past it): a stack with a huge payload stays visible and usable there, as before MP3, and only
    a remote player's view hides it (a push sends it as `!`) (MP3 review, storage m9).
  - **The grid bridge's footprint test on every deposit** (MP3 review, storage M1): a batch's compare-and-sets are judged
    first; then each deposit against the boxes as the batch's earlier entries leave them - a box the batch already writes is
    asked through `RoomAsPlanned`, which lays the planned stacks into the box's own slot array for the one call to
    `ScStorage.BoxHasRoomFor` and puts the box's slot objects back in a `finally` (plain array entries: no event, nothing sent
    or saved, main thread). A deposit no box has room for is answered `-` alone and handed back; the rest of the batch is
    written (MP3 review, storage m10). With the bridge, the view sends a swap (a different item into a backed cell: RE
    Inventory's swap writes it as one cell) as a take and, once that is answered, a deposit - the order the two writes had
    before MP3. A compare-and-set that puts a different item into a slot is footprint-tested too (with that slot emptied).
  - **After the commit** the answer is Done whatever fails: the reply is set right after the writes, `res` is built entry by
    entry with the planned code as the fallback, and the free room, the log and the pushes each have their own try (MP3
    review, storage m1).
  - **The view** (`ScLinkedContainer34`): the window's changes only mark cells; once a frame (`ScStoragePump.LateUpdate`,
    after the click is done, when the cell and the cursor agree) every changed cell goes in one batch, and one batch is in
    flight per view. A refused batch writes nothing; the view puts every cell of that batch, and every cell changed since,
    back to its box as it is now, and settles with the player: what those cells held goes back to the bag and toolbelt (or
    the ground), what they showed before is taken back from the cursor, then the bag and toolbelt (the exact item; netted
    per item first). While a change is on its way the cursor's item cannot be thrown on the ground
    (`XUiC_DragAndDropWindow.DropCurrentItem` is held, and so is `PlaceItemBackInInventory`, the window's own put-back on close
    that throws the stack when the bag is full: the stack stays on the cursor until the answer is in; MP3 review, storage
    m3), so a refused take finds it. On the host AddItem, TryStackItem and
    Sort's merges ask the server first (answered at once) and nothing moves on a refusal, as before; on a client they move at
    once and a refusal gives the items back. The grid bridge's room delegates run on the server only; the view shows the
    server's figures.
  - **Loss and duplication, per cell operation** (the brief's rule 1; "refused" = another view or the world changed the
    slot, the box is gone, locked or open, or there is no room; nothing of a refused batch is written):

    | Operation | Who moves the item | Server changes | Refused | Stale view | Two players | No answer |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Take, take half, shift-click or Take all out of a cell | the player's grid, to the cursor or bag | the batch's commit (slot E to less) | the cell shows the box as it is; the taken items come off the cursor, bag or toolbelt | compare fails: refused | the first batch wins; the second is refused and gives the items back | disconnect only (see below) |
    | Drop, drop one or merge onto a cell with a box slot | the player's grid, from the cursor | commit (slot E to more) | the dropped items go back to the bag (or the ground) | refused | as above | disconnect only |
    | Swap on a cell with a box slot, no grid bridge | the player's grid | commit (slot E to the new item) | the new item goes back to the bag; E comes off the cursor | refused | as above | disconnect only |
    | Swap on a cell with a box slot, grid bridge | the player's grid | first commit: slot E emptied (a take); second: the new item as a deposit | take refused: as a refused take, the new item back to the bag; deposit refused: the new item back to the bag, E stays the player's | take: refused | as above | disconnect only (between the two: the deposit is lost with the view, as any deposit in flight) |
    | Drop on an empty cell (deposit) | the player's grid | commit (the first claimed box whose grid seats it, as the batch leaves the boxes) | that item alone goes back to the bag, the rest of the batch stands | n/a (room is checked then) | each deposit gets its own slot | disconnect only |
    | Shift-click or move-all from the backpack | host: the server first; client: the player's grid | commit | host: nothing moved, the backpack keeps it; client: what found no room is given back, the rest stays in the boxes | n/a | as above | disconnect only |
    | Sort (merging partial stacks) | host: the server first; client: two cells | one commit for both slots | host: nothing moved; client: both cells put back, nothing reaches the player | refused | refused | disconnect only |
    | NPC job deposit or take | the server | at once, pushed to the views that show the slot | the box is skipped (open by hand or locked) | a view's change made before the push is refused | n/a | n/a |
    | Hand-open of a box a view shows | nobody | none (the box leaves the views) | n/a | the view's next change to it is refused | n/a | n/a |

    Every move between the view's own cells nets to zero. A batch is answered on the reliable channel, so "no answer" only
    happens when the client disconnects; then what was in flight may be lost or doubled (the brief's accepted case), and
    after 40 s the view gives up and closes. A death in flight is not a refusal: the batch is held and sent again after the
    respawn (above); a client dead for more than about 2 minutes gets it put back like any refusal. One residual case is
    logged (`could not be taken back`) and not exact: within the round trip of a refused take, the player used, scrapped or
    dropped (through the backpack's own menu) the taken item, and the box no longer holds it either (if it does, the view
    takes it from the box instead, as a fresh change). It needs a conflicting change to that same slot by another view or
    the world within that round trip; in single player it cannot happen.
  - **Known gaps** (MP3 review, storage m5): a sign edit or a lock toggle on a storage box (`TEFeatureSignable`,
    `TEFeatureLockable` call `SetModified`) sends the client's WHOLE tile entity, items included, and the server reads it
    with no lock test (`NetPackageTileEntity.ProcessPackage`). If player A's view commits a change to that box within the
    latency window of player B's edit, B's stale copy overwrites the box: the item is doubled (or a deposit lost). A vanilla
    race between any two players and one box, not one linked storage makes; closing it would take a server-side patch that
    keeps the box's item grid when the sender holds no lock on it (three patches and a per-package sender), so it is left.
    An NPC write into a box's EMPTY slot shows in a view only at its next open or Refresh (a push updates the cells a view
    already shows).
- **Round MP4 (part V), voices (`NetPackageScVoice`; `ScVoice`, "SC's lines on every machine"):** every player in earshot
  hears SC's lines, in the same voice.
  - **The server decides** that a line is said, which one (the clip's key) and in which voice (the voice folder's name), and
    sends it. No path travels: each client finds the file in its own copy (`ScVoice.PathIn`). Recipients:
    - barks, chatter, taunts, small talk and spoken refusals go to every matched remote player within 40 m of the speaker
      (`ScVoice.EarshotRange`, `SendNear`);
    - a greeting, a talk's parting line and a forced console line go to one player (`SendTo`);
    - a radio line goes to every community player (`SendCommunity`); each client judges whether it hears it.
  - **The host's own player is never sent one:** single player and the host play exactly as before. A dedicated server plays
    nothing itself (`ScVoice.PlaysHere`) and counts the speaker busy for about the line (`NoteBusy`), so the next bark waits.
  - The package layout is in `NetPackageScVoice.cs`. Its kinds are bark, talk, small talk, greeting, taunt, horde, radio and
    vc. The caption is SC's words for the key, a Localization key localized on the client, or Voice Control's text.
  - **Greetings** run per player on the server (`ScGreetings`: each player's own memory of who met them today, one at a
    time per player). A remote player's greeting is said on their machine only. On a client, `OwnsGreeting` (Voice
    Control's walk-up greeting skipped) answers from server-synced facts: the copy of the registry for members and
    prospects, and the synced cvars for enclave people and hostility. It answers false when the server runs no SC. So a
    client gets SC's greeting or Voice Control's, never neither.
  - **Taunts** are the server's only (`ScTaunts.Fire`), for raiders near any player. A remote player's death never reaches
    the server's `AwardKill`, so their "killed" comes from the raider that was fighting them, as it lets go of them.
  - **Voice Control's relay, restored:** a line SC says in Voice Control's place on the server (`ScBarks.SubstituteAck`,
    `SubstituteSpeech`) goes to the players within 32 m (`ScVoice.VcRelayRange`), as Voice Control's own TTS relay would
    have. A say-line is the exception (`SubtitleManager.SayToEarshot`, scoped by `Patch_ScSayLineScope`): Voice Control
    sends it to the clients itself, and each substitutes it there.
  - **Radio** (C_world 15): runners' moments (`ScRadioTalk.Say`) and speakers' lines (`ScRadioSpeaker.Say`) are picked once
    on the server and sent to every community player. Each client runs its own queue (`ScRadioTalk.OnRemote`, `ClientTick`)
    with the host's rules: its own handheld radio, a powered station near it, a body beside it. A recall's answer goes to
    the player who recalled.
  - **Horde warnings** in person go to the players within 30 m of the guard. The radio's warnings (`SayAway`, and
    `SayAtHome` beyond 30 m) stay the host's own player's until MP5 (marked `MP5:`).
  - **Talk lines stay local to the talking player** (decided): a dialog's line plays on the machine that has the talk open,
    as before. A client's members now speak in the server's voice there, since `ScVoice.RaceSeed` reads the copy.
  - A line said as a remote player's talk closes goes to that player only (`ScVoice.SayAfterTalkFor`). This covers "That's
    all for now.", "Let me think about it.", a P7 refusal while their talk is open, and an enclave or band answer.
  - Console: `sc voice greet` greets the sender and `sc voice chatter` picks a resident near them. `sc voice barks` and
    `scl voice` end with the network counters.
  - **Known gaps:**
    - a Voice Control line a client speaks itself (its own push-to-talk orders) and that SC substitutes there is heard only
      on that client. Relaying it needs a client-to-server package;
    - `ScWatch` asks `SayAtHome` only with the host's own player, so a dedicated server voices no warning at home until the
      merge passes the anchor (round MP5 part R: done);
    - (until round mt1) SC's config was each machine's own; a client now reads the server's (the pushed block, once
      `ScConfig.TablesReady`), so voice switches agree.

- **Round MP4 (part K), map markers** (`ScMarkers`, `NetPackageScMarkers`, a `CollectMarkers` beside each subsystem's
  `AddMarker`):
  - A map object belongs to one machine: `NavObjectManager.RegisterNavObject` sends nothing, and every SC nav class is
    `IsOwner` (shown only to the object's owner). So each subsystem still draws its markers for this machine's own player
    only (`AddMarker`, owner = the host's own `EntityPlayerLocal`; nothing on a dedicated server), and the server keeps one
    replicated set beside them.
  - **The set:** each `CollectMarkers(List<ScMarkers.Entry>)` lists its markers from the server's state exactly as its
    `AddMarker` draws them: kind, key, nav class, position, name. Kinds: `enclave` (known, not lost), `contact`, `infest`
    (known), `band` (known, not arrived, where they are now), `outpost` (held; the name carries the yield), `opattack` (an
    attack the radio heard), `scout`, `runner` (an open call for help, on her) and `watch` (the horde a guard called out).
    A change to an `AddMarker`'s condition, class or name belongs in its `CollectMarkers` too, and a new marker needs its
    own collector and a line in `ScMarkers.Collect`, or remote players never see it.
  - **The server** (`ScMarkers.ServerTick`, from the ticker once a second, only while a remote client that said hello with
    SC's protocol is on) collects and publishes: a new marker or a new class or name at once; a move once it is
