    `Entry.MoveMin` m from the published spot and `MoveEvery` s after it (bands 10 m and 5 s, as the host's own; a runner's
    call 1.5 m and 1 s; the rest 0.5 m). Each client gets what changed for it through `ScNet.ToPlayer`: the whole set at the
    first tick after its hello, then deltas. A collector that throws keeps its kind's last published markers.
  - **Per viewer (Stage 1):** what the community knows goes to every community player (discovery stays shared until MP5).
    The one viewer rule keeps its form per player: the watch marker (`Entry.NearBase`) goes to a player within
    `BaseAttackLeaveRange` of the home zone.
  - **The client** keeps the server's set and registers each marker in its own `NavObjectManager`, owned by its own
    `EntityPlayerLocal`, so the `IsOwner` classes show it unchanged. `RegisterNavObject` hands back an existing object of
    the same class, owner and spot; one held by a quest of the client's journal or by another marker is never taken over
    (the marker goes 1 cm aside, `ScRunnerCall`'s rule). Every 10 s, and after a package that came before the local player
    existed, the map is squared with the set again; a new world forgets it. Names are written on the server, in its
    language.
  - **The listen host's own map follows** (`ScMarkers.HostFollow`, at most every 3 s, only while remote clients are on):
    when an outpost, enclave or infestation marker comes, goes or is renamed in the server's set, that subsystem's
    `RefreshMarkers` runs for the host's own player. So a remote player's upgrade, repair, Mode or posting renames the host's
    outpost marker, and a client's `sc enclave|infest reveal` shows on the host's map. `ScOutposts.HostMarkersFollow` stays
    for the immediate redraw after the MP2 verbs.
  - Not in the set: quest markers (their own round), and the markers the game makes from an entity on every machine
    (SCore's companion marker, the raiders' clear-sleeper dots, `Patches_Xnpc` X11).
  - **`NetPackageScMarkers`** (server to client): int community (0), bool full, int n, then n changes: byte op (0 put, 1
    move, 2 drop), string kind, string key; a put adds string class and string name; a put or a move adds float x, y, z.

- **Round MP4 (part Q), quests reach the right player's journal** (`ScQuestNet`, `NetPackageScQuestOp`,
  `NetPackageScJournalReport`): a journal belongs to its own machine (`QuestJournal.AddQuest` is local), so the server never
  touches a remote player's journal - it sends a quest op, and the client runs the same `ScQuests` function on its own
  journal. The host's own player keeps its direct path (`ScQuests` on its own journal, as before); single player sends
  nothing.
  - **Who gets what:** a favour quest is the taker's (the dialog starts it on their machine); a favour that ends for
    everyone (a join, a death, a grudge, an enclave person's end) closes every remote copy through
    `ScTalkContext.CloseFavourForOthers`, which now sends the quest op (the talk context's own Close is still read, no
    longer sent). A **rescue** quest goes to every community player online, each their own copy (`ScQuestNet.RescueStarted`
    after `ScRadio.ForceDistress`'s own); `ScQuestNet.CloseRescueAll` closes every copy (the host's own included) whether
    the call was won (the first to complete it completes it for everyone), lost or ended. An **enclave request's** quest is
    the accepting player's: `EnclaveRequest.AcceptedBy` (the platform id, saved as `acceptedby=`, never in a client's copy;
    empty - every save from before MP4 - is the host's own player). `ScQuestNet.StartRequestFor` starts it on the host's
    journal or sends it; `ScEncRequests.Close` / `Reshape` reach a remote acceptor with `CloseRequestRemote` /
    `ShapeRequestRemote`; the host's once-a-session `SyncJournal` keeps only the requests the host accepted
    (`HostAccepted`). A siege's quest is the player it was called for. Until MP5 opens the enclave dialogs, every acceptance
    is the host's.
  - **The op** (server to one client): byte version, byte kind (0 favour close, 1 favour re-key, 2 rescue start, 3 rescue
    close, 4 rescue re-key, 5 request start, 6 request shape, 7 request close), int id, int new id (re-keys), bool completed
    (closes), then whole numbers and strings by kind - a rescue's box and names; a request's place, size, due day and flags,
    and its quest class, kind and names (`ScQuests.RequestQuestData`, which the host's own `StartRequest` / `ShapeRequest`
    are made from too, so both build the same quest). Titles are made on the receiving machine, in its language; a
    request's "what" line is the server's words.
  - **The report** (client to server, when it changed and every 30 s; `ScQuestNet`'s client tick, hooked into GameUpdate
    from `ScTalkContext.Reset`): the NPC being talked to, then each SC quest (POI favour, supplies favour, rescue, request:
    its id, an enclave person's id, in progress, past the clearing), then the POIs every quest in progress leads to. The
    server keeps the last one per player for the session and answers: a POI favour past its clearing is done (what
    `SyncCompleted` does for the host: `Done`, `sc_favourDone` force-sent, the tell - not while they talk to that survivor);
    a favour quest whose prospect is gone closes, an enclave person's follows its new key; a rescue or request quest that
    ended while they were away closes (the session's outcome, else removed); an open rescue their journal lacks starts (once
    per player per call - one removed by hand stays removed), as does a request they accepted (once a session, and its
    quest is shaped to the record once a session). E6: a POI favour a remote report held this session that no player holds
    any more (no report, not the host's journal) has `Started` reset, so its building's vanilla clear quests re-arm for
    remote players again; a report holding one marks it taken on again, and `ScQuests.IsOurPoi` also answers from a remote
    subscriber's last report. The pickers that keep away from the player's quest POIs (enclaves, infestations, a missing
    survivor's building) add the reported POIs (`ScQuestNet.AddReportedPois`).
  - **Markers on a client:** the client tick runs `ScQuests.TickMarkers` on its own journal every second, from its copy's
    prospect positions (a POI favour's marker follows the survivor from phase 2, a lost request marker is placed again), and
    `EnsureMapObject` places the supplies favour's marker from the copy (MP1 had left both off on a client).
  - **Known gaps:** (the distress call on a dedicated server and its trigger and judging by any community player: round MP4
    review fixes, below.) The session tables (who got which rescue, how calls
    and requests ended, who held which favour) are not saved: after a server restart a copy that ended meanwhile closes as
    removed, and a favour held only by a player who has not reported since the restart can lose `Started` if another holder
    drops it first (their POI may then be re-armed once). A request accepted by a player whose platform id changed is no
    longer theirs.

- **Round MP4 (part: tells), tells in each player's language** (`ScCommunity.TellKey`, `ScPlayers.TellCommunityKey`,
  `NetPackageScTell`): a tell used to reach a remote player as the server's finished words (`GameManager.ShowTooltipMP`:
  the server's language, and vanilla's toolbelt package drops the sound). A tell made from a localization row now goes by
  its key: `ScCommunity.TellKey(player, "scRunBack", sound, m.Name, yield, ScCommunity.LocArg(ScNames.LocKey(kind)))`, or
  `ScPlayers.TellCommunityKey(key, sound, args)` for every community player online.
  - **The arguments** are the row's, exactly as `string.Format(Localization.Get(key), args)` took them; one that is itself
    a localization key (a resource name, a status word) goes as `ScCommunity.LocArg(key)` and is localized where the tell
    shows. Names and a helper's words (`Hours`, `Side`, `Dist`, a place's "where", a standing word) are still the server's
    text: only the row and the `LocArg`s change language.
  - **The host's own player** (single player too) gets `Tell(player, ScCommunity.TellText(key, args), sound)`: the same text
    the old call site made, byte for byte. A row with no arguments is shown as it is, not run through `string.Format`.
  - **A remote player whose SC said hello with this protocol** (`ScNet.ClientMatches`) gets a `NetPackageScTell` through
    `ScNet.ToPlayer`, and their machine formats the row with its own `Localization` (`ScCommunity.ShowTellKey`; a row its
    language formats badly shows as it is) and plays the sound. A vanilla client, another SC protocol, or a tell with more
    than `NetPackageScTell.MaxArgs` (8) arguments gets the server's words, as before.
  - **`NetPackageScTell`** (server to one client): byte version, string key, string sound, byte argc, then per argument a
    byte kind (0 text, 1 localization key, 2 int32, 3 int64, 4 float, 5 double) and the value. Numbers travel as numbers so
    the client's `string.Format` applies the row's own format (`{1:0.0}`) as the host's does; every machine runs the game's
    one invariant culture (`Utils.InitStatic`), so they format alike. Anything else travels as `string.Format("{0}", x)`.
  - **New tells:** use `TellKey` / `TellCommunityKey` whenever the text is one localization row. Keep `Tell(player, text,
    sound)` for text built otherwise: two rows joined (a radio "says" line plus its news), a helper's text (`ErrorText`,
    `InfoText`, `DropWhy`), a verb's finished refusal, and an `ScActionReply`'s text (a reply already carries a key the
    client localizes, `ScActionReply.Text`). The file-local broadcasters have key twins: `ScBaseAttack.TellInFightKey`,
    `ScBands.TellNearKey`, `ScEnclavePeople.TellNearKey`, `ScWatch.TellHomeKey`, `ScDowned.TellAboutKey`.
  - **Not tells:** MP4 part V's captions (`NetPackageScVoice`) and the quest titles (part Q) were already localized on the
    client; marker names (part K) are still the server's words.

- **Round cfs1, Craft from Storage in the base** (`ScCraftFromBase`, `Patch_CraftFromBase`): a postfix on 0-SCore's
  `RemoteCraftingUtils.GetTileEntities(EntityAlive)` - the one-argument overload that only its crafting paths call, for the
  ingredient counts (`SearchNearbyContainers`) and the taking (`ConsumeItem`) alike - adds, while the player stands inside the
  home zone, every box linked storage links there (`ScStorage.Linkable`, minus boxes locked against the player), ignoring
  0-SCore's Distance and broadcast list. 0-SCore's enemy rule and its open-by-another-player skip still apply. (Round t2 moved
  the hook to the three-argument overload, which upgrades' and repairs' counts and takes use too - see "Round t2" below; the
  drop box keeps 0-SCore's range.) On a client the zone comes from the mirror and the boxes are the client's loaded
  copies; 0-SCore's taking on a client writes the whole box back to the server (its own multiplayer model, unchanged here).
  `CraftFromBaseStorage` (default true) turns it off. (A client's open-box skip and range: round MP4 review fixes, below.)

- **Round MP4 (review fixes)** (the round's three code reviews):
  - **Rescues for every player** (markers/quests M1, M2): `ScRadio.RollDistress` no longer needs a local player, and
    `TickDistress` spawns and judges a call by `RescuerNear` (the anchor first, as before, else the first living community
    player within range), so any player's copy can be the one completed; a run's arrival with any player beside her is an
    escort, not a distress call (`ScRunners`). A client's copy from an earlier call or session
    (not in `rescueSent`) is closed and started again for the open call (m1). A rescue quest has its own `QuestCode` and
    `QuestUniqueId` (m4).
  - **Request quests:** `AcceptedBy` stays empty for the host's own player (m2), so a save loaded under another platform id
    keeps its quest (no deployed build ever wrote the host's id). A siege quest that reached no journal is logged (m5).
  - **Voices:** `ScVoiceRoutes.SayRefusal` takes the talker from its call site (`TalkFollow`'s sender) instead of guessing it
    from SCore's sticky `CurrentNPC` cvar (voices M1), and tests a remote talker before the host's own talk. The "all clear" goes to the tick's player
    (m6). The host's open talk keeps chatter away from the host only (m2). A client drops a server line (not a taunt) while
    its own Voice Control speaks on that NPC (m3). A client hears the base's stations from its copy of the registry (m5). A
    raider's "killed" taunt is tested before a new living target (m7). A Voice Control line SC says in its place is not
    relayed when it is the say-line just said on that NPC - its line id or its words, within 3 s (`ScBarks.NoteSayLine`, m8;
    any other line on her, an order's ack, still is). A dedicated server does not count a horde line as
    "unheard" (tells n5).
  - **Craft from Storage:** the server sends its clients the home zone's open boxes (`NetPackageScOpenBoxes`: int32
    community, int32 n, n x int32 x, y, z; the whole set when it changes, at most once a second, and to a client that lacks
    it) and a client's crafting leaves them out, 0-SCore's own near ones too when they are base boxes (cfs1 M1). A client adds no base box beyond
    150 m (m1). SC's own takes from the bag (`ScItemTx.TakeFromLocal`, `ScCategories`, `ScTalkContext`) run inside
    `ScCraftFromBase.SuppressContainers`, so 0-SCore's `ConsumeItem` sees no container there (m5). The base's box list is
    worked out once a frame per player (n8).
  - **Tells:** an enclave's standing word, an ally's benefit name and a band's kind word go as `LocArg` (m4). The host's
    own tell shows a badly formatted row as it is, as a client does (n1). `NetPackageScTell.read` keeps at most 8 arguments (n7).
  - **Known gaps (left on purpose):**
    - Craft from Storage on a client: an open the client has not heard of yet (up to a second), and a server-side write to
      a box in the same instant as a client's craft (an NPC deposit, another player's linked storage commit), can still be
      overwritten by the client's whole-box write. A server-side take (a request with compare-and-set, as linked storage
      does) would close it: a round of its own. A linked storage view is not told of a craft's take until its next change
      to that slot is refused and resyncs (cfs1 m2). A box the client itself just closed stays left out until the next set
      (up to a second). A box outside the base, or not one linked storage links, keeps 0-SCore's own client behavior.
    - A remote player's weapon pick in a talk reaches the server as Voice Control's swap (route "voice"), so its refusal is
      said to everyone in earshot, not as that talk's parting line; the talking client drops it while its talk is open on her.
    - On a dedicated server a distress call needs a powered station at home: a remote player's handheld is not readable there.
    - A greeting a client drops (its own talk open) is still spent on the server (voices m1). The greeting switches are
      each machine's own, so server off and client on gives no greeting at all (m4). A non-member's say-lines and talk
      lines on a client can be in another voice than the one the server names (m9). The greeting memory is not pruned
      within a session (n4).
    - A forged journal report can mark a POI favour done or taken on (quests m3): the same trust the game gives a client's
      journal. A siege's quest is the anchor's alone until MP5 (m5). Marker labels, place names and a helper's words in a
      tell (`Where`, `DestText`, `Describe`, a reason) stay in the server's language (m6, tells m4).
- **Round mt1 (part C), a client's config and game-start steps:** on a client `ModEvents.GameStartDone` fires right after
  `StartAsClient`, before the server's configs (blocks, items, dialogs, ...) arrive. `ScConfig.TablesReady` is true on a
  server; on a client it is `WorldStaticData.AllConfigsReceivedAndLoaded() && World != null` (the test vanilla's
  `worldInfoCo` waits on before it creates the world; the world test covers the main menu, where the first answers true
  from the last session). While it is false, `ScConfig.EnsureParsed` returns without parsing and without latching
  `Parsed` (one `config: ... is read once the server's blocks are in` line), so the next call reads the server's block;
  once it is true a missing block latches the defaults as before. The GameStartDone body is now
  `SurvivorCommunityModApi.OnGameStartDone` (same steps, same order): a server runs it from GameStartDone; a client's is
  held (`ScConfig.HoldStartDone`) and run once from the GameUpdate handler, first in it, by `ScConfig.StartDoneDue` (a bool
  test, then at most one readiness test a second). In practice that is the client's first game update, when its player is
  created. The hold is dropped at GameStarting (`ScConfig.Reset`) and at world end. Each step is safe to run then: they
  only read the tables, and the ones that cache (bundle pools, the meds mix, the well kit's cut, the L&L binding, Vanity
  Vault's binding) build once per world after the parse. Between a client's GameStartDone and its world, SC runs only
  block, item and XUi loading code, and none of it reads `ScConfig`. Every lazily built cache that reads it
  (`ScXnpc`'s class sets, `ScLoadout`'s catalogue, `ScVoice`'s table, `ScCategories`) calls `EnsureParsed` first and is
  first built after the world exists, when that parses for real. The late `config has no XnpcWeapons block` warning was
  the same fault: `XnpcWeapons` is a class inside the config block, and the early latch left it empty. A new cache built
  from `ScConfig` must not be built on a client before `TablesReady`, or it keeps the defaults.

- **Round mt1 (part H), a remote talker's talk hold** (`ScWork`, `ScTalkContext`, `NetPackageScTalkContext`): 0-XNPCCore's
  hired rows (SCore's `Leader` requirement), its craft list and Voice Control's server-side router (`NpcRelation.IsLeaderOfEntity`,
  SCore's `GetLeaderOrOwner`) all need the talking player as her leader or owner, and a resident has neither. Round 28's talk
  hold gives her that link, the `Owner` cvar, but it started from a postfix on the dialog window, which runs on the machine
  whose dialog opens - so only ever for the host's own player.
  - **Start:** when a client's talk context `Open` reaches the server (`ScTalkContext.OnTalkOpen`), after its checks, a
    member goes through `ScWork.HoldForRemote`: a new hold through `StartTalk` (now any `EntityPlayer`; `CanHold`'s rules), or
    the talker's own hold or shift already, before the facts are built and sent, so the force-sent `Owner` is queued first.
    The facts carry `Linked` (flag bit 5). A client whose copy of `Owner` has not landed yet re-evaluates the talk's rows once
    it shows its own entity id (polled for 3 s).
  - **Lifetime:** the client runs the host's own window test (`ScWork.TalkOpen`, which reads only the local player's UI) and
    sends `Held` (kind 3: int32 npc, byte flags, bit 0 the dialog with her is open) every second while it holds and through
    `TalkGraceSeconds` after, then `TalkClosed` (kind 4: int32 npc). The package's version byte is 2. The server keeps a
    remote hold (`Shift.Remote`) on those: it ends on the close, after 5 s without a beat, when the player is gone or more
    than `TalkRange` (20 m) from her, or at the 600 s cap since the dialog was last open. Nothing for a remote hold reads or
    closes the host's windows. Her first job or craft order promotes it to a work shift exactly as in single player
    (`ScWork.Tick` / `Promote`, all on the server), and the resident rule leaves her alone while any shift holds her.
  - **The end of a link** goes to the right player: `End` passes a remote owner to `ScCommunity.Station` as the party she
    leaves, a disconnect ends that player's holds and shifts (`ScWork.ForgetPlayer`, from `PlayerDisconnected`; not at a
    shutdown), and the crates-full / pack-full tells go to the shift's player. On the client, once her copy names it no more,
    the local hire mark (`hired_<id>`, which SCore's leader component stamps on a client too), leader cache and companion
    marker for her go.
  - **Two players: the first holder keeps her.** A second talker (a client, or the host's own player) gets no link and a
    tell (`scTalkBusyWith`); another player's Call to work is refused while someone else's talk holds her
    (`scWorkHeldOther`). Since the review fixes, SC's own verbs are refused the same way, and an idle hold yields (below).
  - **`Leader` reaches clients when it ends:** `ScCommunity.Detach`, `CleanupStale` and `ScRunners.ApplyTravelOrder` clear
    `Leader` (and `Owner`) through `ScCommunity.ClearSynced`, so a client no longer keeps the player who last led her as her
    leader after she is sent home (its rows showed, and every request was refused). Any code that ends a link must do the same.
  - **Known gaps:** a client's leftover `hired_<id>` may already have reached the server's copy of that player through the
    player's own cvar sync before the client drops it (SCore's `CheckForDanglingHires` prunes it at the next check). A late,
    unreliable bulk buff sync built before a `ClearSynced` could in theory bring the old `Leader` / `Owner` back on a client
    (needs the network to reorder packages; not settled by reading); a new talk with her corrects `Owner`.
  - **Review fixes:** **the craft window's Cancel on a client** (`Patches_Craft` C1n `ClientCancel`): for a resident whose copy
    shows her linked to this player (`Owner`) or on a shift (`sc_shift`), it sends `member.canceljob` (member; Community; the
    server checks a loaded member, the sender within 40 m and that her hold or shift is theirs, `ScWork.OwnedBy`, then runs
    `ScWork.CancelJobOnShift`; hint `dropped`), relists on the answer and skips the original's `VCCommand.Stay` - her shift
    and link survive as on the host. **When the server ends a client's link** (`ScTalkContext.HeldTick`, her `Owner` no
    longer names it), the client closes its windows on her - `ScWork.CloseTalkWindows(npc)`, the list the host's cap branch
    closes, factored out - and, while the dialog with her is still open, sends one `Open` (at most every 10 s per member) so
    the server links her again if she is free. **A follow** force-sends `Leader` and `Owner` right after
    `ScNpc.SetLeaderAndOwner` (`FollowInternal`), so a client reads the follow in the same frame as the shift's `Owner` 0.
    **The client's hire-mark sweep** takes the mark off at once when her copy is no longer loaded (not a follower's), takes
    members whose facts say "not linked", and scans the mirror's residents every 2 s for an `Owner` naming the local player
    (Call to work, a shift) without `Leader`. **Two players** (`ScWork.HandOverSeconds` = 90 s): the first holder keeps her
    until her talk hold has stood that long with no order (still a Talk phase; a work shift never yields). Until then a second
    talker gets `scTalkBusyWith`, and another player's Call to work and SC's own verbs - `member.follow`, `member.home`,
    `member.leave`, `member.run`, `member.post`, `member.watch` - are refused with `scWorkHeldOther` (`ScWork.RefuseHeldByOther`).
    After it, a second player's talk or Call to work (`HandOver`: the host's own through the talk-hold postfix too) ends
    the idle hold (`YieldIdleTalk`: no walk back, the host's own windows on her closed), tells its player `scTalkHandedOver`
    ("{0} is with {1} now."), and goes on; one of those verbs (and the Facilities tab's member picks, and a listen host's
    spoken "follow me") is simply no longer refused (`IdleTalk`): the verb's own success moves her on, so a verb its own
    checks refuse never costs the first player the hold (re-check F1, F2, F4; with another player online, `EndFor` ending the host's own talk hold closes the host's windows on her - also when the host gave the verb itself, a known cosmetic gap); that player's quiet re-ask within 15 s gets no "busy" line. `member.workend` never
    ends another player's talk hold (`ScWork.TalkHeldByOther`). **A player who leaves while she is down** marks the kept hold
    or shift (`Shift.OwnerGone`), and `Tick` ends it once she is up.

- **Round MP5 (part E), enclave dialogs for every player** (`ScActionsEnclave`, `ScEncFacts`, `ScEncTalk`, `ScTalkContext`,
  `NetPackageScTalkContext`, `DialogOpsEnclaves`, `DialogOpsEncRequests`): any community player talks to an enclave's people -
  trade, "Can you come with me?", "Would you join us?" (with their favour), gifts, the threat, "Would your people join ours?",
  the requests (accept, hand over, a gun from the pick, decline, think, give up, "I found ...", "I already took care of it."),
  a missing survivor's follow and wait, and "Search the body" on their remains. The enclave's standing, trust, known flags,
  requests and cooldowns stay the community's (Stage 1; `MP6:` markers where they become per community).
  - **The host's own talk is unchanged:** every enclave operator keeps its code for the server (single player, the listen
    host's own player) and runs it inline as before. The work of each action moved into cores both paths share
    (`ScEncRequests.AcceptAs` / `DeclineAs` / `CantDoAs` / `FoundAs` / `ClearedAs` / `ThinkAs`, `ScEnclaveDeals.EnlistPaid`,
    `ScEncSearch.FollowAt` / `WaitAt` / `SearchAtFor`, `ScEncJoin.Join` and `ScEnclaveDeals.Recruit` with an `EntityPlayer`),
    and each returns the reply line's key and specifics instead of writing the host's `reply` static.
  - **The talk's facts (version 3 of `NetPackageScTalkContext`; layout in its header):** when a client's talk opens, and
    again after each enclave action that changes what a row reads (`ScTalkContext.SendFactsAfter`, sent before that action's answer: the reliable
    channel keeps order), the server adds what the enclave rows need (`ScEncFacts.Fill`, from the host's own requirement code
    answered for that player): `EncFlags` (alive, leader / member / enlisted, leader talking, can trade, the show halves of
    the enlist, recruit, gift and join rows, needs a favour, can give up, a thanks to spend, an infestation to make known),
    the standing key, the kind, each greyed row's reason without the halves that read the player's own bag (the enlist fee's
    dukes, the gift's bundles), the fee, the open request (id, state, kind, stage, resource, units), the found row's words,
    a missing survivor's stage, where their leader is, and the line's tokens (`ScEncToken`: a localization key the client
    localizes - the voiced request lines, the standing word, the kind - and the server's words for the specifics). The
    favour row's roll (`ScEnclaveDeals.EnsureFavour`) happens there under the host's own rule. The tokens are made with no
    side effect (`ScEncRequests.AskParts`, `WaitParts`, `NoneKey`); the line's own effects - the offer's infestation made
    known, the leader's thanks spent - are the client's to report when its line shows them (`enc.seen`).
  - **A client's rows** (`ScEncTalk`) read the facts and its own bag and journal (the dukes, the bundles carried, the
    request's items, its gift and gun picks, its favour quest's verdict) and change nothing. Its line (`ScEncDialog.Fill` ->
    `ScEncTalk.Fill`) fills the tokens in the host's order and `{where}` from its own position. A trade window's prices scale
    by its copy of the enclave's standing (`ScEnclaveDeals.ScalePrice`: the body's `%sc_enc`).
  - **The reply line:** an action whose next statement says the leader's answer (`{reqreply}`, `{giftreply}`) shows "..."
    (`scEncReplyWait`) until the answer comes; then `ScTalkContext.RefillLine` swaps the statement on screen for a twin (the
    same id, text, owner and rows - vanilla makes such statements itself for a quest offer), so the line panel fills it again
    and its voice says it. The dialog's own statements are never written. A line filled before the facts came (a talk
    reopened at a statement, after the trade window) is filled again when they land.
  - **Ops** (`ScActionsEnclave`, Community access; ints unless marked s:; `npc` = the NPC's entity id, loaded, alive and within
    `ScTalkContext.TalkRange` of a remote sender): `enc.threat` (npc; hint `give`: the bundles), `enc.enlist` (npc, paid; s: the
    dukes), `enc.gohome` (npc), `enc.recruit` (npc, journal: 1 when the client's POI favour quest is past its clearing; hints
    `fclose`/`fdone`), `enc.join` (npc), `enc.gift` (npc; s: the bundle), `enc.req` (npc, what: 1 accept, 2 decline, 3 think, 4
    can't do, 5 found, 6 cleared), `enc.reqgive` (npc; s: the stacks), `enc.reqgun` (npc; s: the gun), `enc.follow` (npc),
    `enc.wait` (npc), `enc.seen` (npc, what: 1 the infestation, 2 the thanks; quiet); "Search the body" is part W's
    `encremains.search`, which runs `ScEncSearch.SearchAtFor` for the sender. A reply line comes back as hints `rk` (the key, localized on the client) and `ri` (the specifics,
    URI-escaped, in the server's language).
  - **Items (round MP3's rule):** a hand-over leaves the client's bag first - the enlist fee by the currency's type, a gift's
    bundle and a request's bundles by type (`ScItemTx.TakeFromLocal`, the least overpay as the host's `Choose`), a generator
    and a gun from their exact slot (`ScEncRequests.TakeForRequest`, `TakeGun`) - and travels encoded in the request's strings;
    the server checks it against the record now (the fee at the standing now, bundles of the asked resource covering the units,
    one generator, one gun that counts; co-op trust in the stacks themselves) and keeps it, or refuses and the client gives it
    back (`ScItemTx.GiveToLocal`: the bag, else the feet). The threat's bundles are made on the server and come back in the
    answer (`ScEnclavePeople.Threaten` takes any player and the remote list, which `ScStockpile.GiveBundles` fills). A supplies favour's hand-over is round MP2's two steps, and
    the row's recruit waits for it (`ScTalkContext.EncAfterHandOver`).
  - **Quests and tells:** a request a client accepts is theirs (`EnclaveRequest.AcceptedBy`: `ScQuestNet.StartRequestFor` sends
    them the quest, its closes and reshapes follow it); the join's and the recruit's favour quests close on the acting player's
    own journal (the quest op, or the reply's `fclose`), everyone else's through `ScTalkContext.CloseFavourForOthers`. Tells go
    to the acting player by key. `sc enclave request <id> accept`, `sc enclave gift <id>` and `sc enclave join <id> now` act for
    the player who typed them.
  - **Known gaps:** the greyed rows' reasons, the request lines' specifics, the found row and `{joinfacts}` / `{terms}` /
    `{benefit}` are the server's words (its language), as a tell's helper words are; the voiced keys and the standing and kind
    words are the client's. A client's rows show what the facts said at the talk's open or its last action (a day passing in
    the talk, another player's action meanwhile, can leave a row one step behind; every action checks everything again). A
    hand-over whose answer never comes (a dropped connection) is lost to the player. Any community player may send an
    enlisted person home or tell a missing survivor to wait (one shared community). An enclave's containers refuse a client
    too, from its copy (`Patches_Enclaves.Patch_ContainerLock`; the server does not check it again; a lost enclave's old
    footprint still refuses there, as the copy never holds `Lost`). A remote player who goes offline keeps the people they
    enlisted for 5 minutes while the body stays in the world (another player near keeps its chunk); then they go home, the
    fee spent. **Known gap:** a lone client's disconnect unloads the body, which ends the deal at once (`OnUnbodied`, no
    refund); keeping it would need the body rebuilt beside the returning player.
- **Round MP5 (part R), the radio for every player** (`ScActionsRadio`, `ScRadioView`, `XUiC_ScRadio`, `ScRadio`,
  `Patches_Radio`, `ScEncCalls`, `ScEncSiege`, `ScHordeVoice`, `ScRadioTalk`): any community player uses the radio station
  (and, with `HandheldRadioTab` on, the Radio tab over their own handheld radio). A client opens the window on its own
  machine (`ScRadio.Open`: its copy of the block's power; no more scHostOnly) and every button calls `ScActions.Request` on
  every machine - inline on the host, which shows the answer exactly as the old inline call's result.
  - **Ops** (whole numbers; every op starts with `mode, x, y, z`: mode 0 at a station, x y z its block - the window's
    `ScRadio.CurrentRadio` -, mode 1 over a handheld): `radio.view` (+ barter give, get, ally id, ally give, ally get,
    runner id; quiet), `radio.drop`, `radio.call`, `radio.scout` (+ resource), `radio.barter` (+ give, get), `radio.demand`
    (+ outpost id seen, pay 1/0), `radio.siege` (+ 1 on our way / 2 can't come, siege id seen), `radio.allytrade` (+ enclave
    id, give, get), `radio.allycall` (+ enclave id, 0 fighter / 1 drop), `radio.recall` (+ member id). Access Community. For a
    remote sender the server checks a station request against the world (a `BlockScRadio` there, the sender within
    `ScActionsRadio.StationReach`, 10 m) and keeps it as theirs (`ScRadio.NoteStationFor` / `StationFor`: the per-player
    `CurrentRadio`); `ScRadio.Usable(who)` and the verbs' `Can*` take the player (`ScBarter`, `ScScouting`, `ScEncBarter`,
    `ScEncCalls`). A handheld request is trusted (the bag is the client's: it closes its window when the radio leaves it)
    while `HandheldRadioTab` is on; a station control over a handheld is refused while the home station is offline. The
    seen ids refuse a stale press (another demand: scActionChanged; another or no siege: the verb's "That call is over.").
    `OpeningAtBlock` stays a machine-local UI flag (the window's own opening).
  - **The view** (`radio.view`, polled by a client every 1.5 s while open, one at a time, again at once after a pick or an
    act): what the copy lacks - each control's readiness and reason (the server's words), the sandbox's drop days, the
    barter terms, the picked ally's trade/fighter/drop and the call that is on, the siege's id, clock and "on our way", the
    picked runner's recall - as `key=value` hints with `%`, `;` and `=` escaped (`ScActionsRadio.Esc`), parsed once per
    answer into `ScRadioView`. Each answer carries the picks it was made for; a binding whose pick changed shows "not ready"
    until the next. The lists (contacts, outposts, distress, enclaves, requests, allies, runners, the siege) come from the
    copy (`ScRegistry.Ready`). A client's station window closes beyond `StationReach` (minus a meter).
  - **Near the caller:** the radio's air drop flies to the player who asked (`ScRadio.DropFor`; `Patches_Radio` runs
    vanilla's `SpawnAirDrop` with an `AIAirDrop` of that one player - single player: the one player, as before). An ally's
    fighter and supply drop come to the caller (`ScEncCalls` keeps the caller's platform id per call for the session; the
    fighter is enlisted with them; caller dead: it waits, as before; offline, or a call loaded from a save: the anchor).
    A radio contact is rolled at the caller's gamestage at a POI picked around home, as before. A client's contact and
    scout markers reach a listen host's own map at once (and the client's through `ScMarkers`).
  - **`RadioOnlyAirdrops`** holds on a dedicated server too while any community player is online who is not known to run
    another SC version (`ScNet.KnownMismatch`; a joining player counts before their hello, so an overdue vanilla drop does
    not fly to them at login). With nobody online, or only mismatched clients, vanilla's schedule runs.
  - **Sieges:** called for any community player in range (the anchor first, as before: `ScEncSiege.PickSource`); the quest
    is that player's. Not every player's: a request keeps one acceptor and `ScQuestNet`'s journal report removes a request
    quest from any other journal - a copy per player needs `ScQuestNet` to track a siege's holders as it tracks a rescue's.
    `ScEncSiege.StatusFor` (the quest's countdown) now answers on a client from its copy.
  - **Voices:** horde warnings reach a client's radio. `ScHordeVoice.SayAtHome` sends to the remote players near the guard
    and, when the guard can send (their handheld, the home station with power), to those at home; `SayAway` (now any
    player) to the remote players away, once per moment and frame. The server marks such a line `ScRadioTalk.FlagHordeRadio`
    (bit 6 of `NetPackageScVoice`'s flags byte, which that file leaves free); the client hears it in person beside the guard,
    else on its radio while it carries a handheld (`ScRadioTalk.OnRemote`, `RadioHeard`). Speakers' and runners' radio lines
    were already sent to every community player (round MP4).
  - **Without an id on the record, after a load an ally's call comes to the anchor** (the save's `call=` has no caller).
  - **At the merge:** `ScBaseAttack` (the call and the arrival) and `ScDefence` (the result) pass their player to
    `ScHordeVoice.SayAway`, so a dedicated server voices the radio from home too (the host's own player is judged as
    before; the remote players away get the line once). `radio.demand` passes the sender to `ScOutpostRisk.PayDemand` /
    `RefuseDemand` (any player since part O), so a client's refusal starts the raid with its radio call and the raiders'
    answer, as the host's does.
  - **Known gaps:** a client's reasons and the barter / scout lines are the server's words (its language); a "missing
    person" request says "someone" on a client (enclave people are not in the copy). The handheld is trusted. A
    per-player request budget is still not kept. MP6: cooldowns, contacts, allies and the siege per community.
- **Round MP5 (part W), the world around any player** (`ScEnclaves`, `ScInfestations`, `ScBands`, `ScEnclavePeople`,
  `ScBaseAttack`, `ScEncMoves`, `ScEncRaids`, `ScLeadership`, `ScActionsWorld`, `BlockScEncRemains`): the world ticks still get
  the anchor (`ScPlayers.Anchor()`), but every "is a player there" now asks for any community player, with the anchor tried
  first - so single player and a host alone take exactly the old branch.
  - **Discovery:** an unknown enclave or infestation is found by the first community player within its discover range (the
    anchor first, alive or not as before; the others living). The known flag stays the community's; the finder is told, the
    host's own map gets the marker whoever found it (`AddMarker(e, ScCommunity.LocalPlayer)`), remote maps through `ScMarkers`.
    With no home, the radio's reveal (enclaves) and report (infestations) also hears a place in range of any community player
    when none is in range of the anchor, and each player is told where it is from where they stand (the same per-player
    "where" in `ScEncMoves.Move`'s told move). The infestation roll's weighting still measures from the anchor.
  - **Bands:** met when any player sees them or a community player comes within `KnowRange` (the one who met them is told);
    trouble starts round the community player near them (the anchor first), at that player's gamestage, its thanks for the
    nearest one; no body comes while a fight is on near the community player nearest them. Raiders (and drifters against the
    community) engage whichever community player one of them sees within `AggroRange`, the anchor first, and keep that player
    (`Band.EngagedId`, session only) while they live and stay within `BandDespawnRange`, then take the next one seen. Drifters
    turned on the community hold ScTrust's grudge against every community player online (Band.Trust is the community's;
    MP6: a per-target grudge). With no home, a dedicated server's day roll sends a band past a community player drawn at random
    (the anchor on a listen host, as before). `sc band` acts round its sender (`ScBands.Command(p, into, me)`).
  - **A talk from any player halts a band:** `ScBands.InTalk` keeps the host's own dialog test and adds the server's talk lock:
    SCore's `EntityAliveSDX.OnEntityActivated` takes `LockManager`'s channel-0 lock on the NPC (shared, so several players may
    hold it) and the dialog's close lets it go (`EntityTrader.TransitionToNextWindow`), so the server reads
    `LockManager.sharedLocks` for any holder but its own player. No package. The band's talk rows were already a client's (round
    MP2, part M: the talk context and `band.join`).
  - **The home's horde:** `ScBaseAttack.InvolvedPlayer(anchor)` is the player whose gamestage sizes and mixes it - the strongest
    community player near the base, else the strongest online, else the anchor (on a client: the given player);
    `StrongestOnline` is the second half. `Size`, the spawn mix and `ScSiege.Besiege` use it, and `ScEncRaids.LoseHeld` sizes an
    unattended raid's loss with `StrongestOnline`. A held attack counts Leadership's defend event for every community player at
    the fight (`ScLeadership.Count(cvar, player)`; `Count(cvar)` is the host's own, the anchor on a dedicated server).
    `ScLeadership.Publish` gives each player their own follower count (a follower counts for the player its record names as
    leader when online, else for the anchor). Presence ("at home", the live fight, joining an away fight, the siege's "home for
    the blood moon", lights out's dawn tell, whole-group arrivals) was already any community player (round MP0).
  - **A world tick with no player:** a dedicated server whose last player left lets go of the enclave buildings it held loaded
    (`ScEnclavePeople.IdleTick`, hooked into `ModEvents.GameUpdate` once per process; ScTicker returns while nobody is online),
    so their chunks unload and each body is recorded by the unload capture. Bodies, doors and streamer names otherwise come
    round any online player since round MP0.
  - **Op** (`ScActionsWorld`): `encremains.search` (x, y, z; Community): "Search the body" on a missing survivor's remains,
    asked on every machine (inline for the host's own player). A remote sender must be alive within `RemainsReach` (8 m) of a
    remains block. The search is part E's `ScEncSearch.SearchAtFor`, which tells the sender (`scEncRemainsNothing` /
    `Searched` / `Found`) on any machine.
  - **Console:** `sc infest add|clear|spread|roll`, `sc band ...`, `sc enclave move <id> now` and `sc enclave raid <id> ...` act
    for the sender (`ConsoleCmdSc.Me`). `sc enclave raid ... now` refuses the demand for the sender through
    `ScOutpostRisk.RefuseDemand`. `ScEncRaids.OnPaid(o, by)` takes the payer (optional).
  - **Left as they were:** `sc enclave join` (`ScEncJoin.Command`, the dialog join's round), the late seed's view rule and `ScEnclaves.InView`
    (the server's loaded chunks already cover every remote player).
  - **Leadership counters** (`ScLeadership.Count`) add 1 with `CVarOperation.add`, so a remote player's machine, which drains
    them, gets exactly one per event. **Known gap:** `ScLeadership.Read` (the leadership multipliers) on a dedicated server
    reads the anchor's server-side copy of `$`-named cvars, which follows the client's own only through the stats sync
    (every few seconds): a lag, not a loss.
- **Round MP5 (part N), physical runners around every player** (`ScRunners`, `ScRuns`, `ScMining`, `ScRunnerCall`, the
  distress functions of `ScRadio`, `ScGarrison`'s posting travel). A physical runner - a supply or mining run, a member
  walking to or from an outpost - used to be captured and re-created around ONE player (the host's own, or the tick's
  anchor on a dedicated server). Now every community player with a chunk observer on the server counts: the host's own, and
  each remote player's server-side observer (the server gives every client one, with that client's own view distance).
  No new op or package: the bodies reach clients by vanilla's entity sync (80 m), the lines by MP4's tells, voices and
  markers.
  - **The rules** (private helpers in `ScRunners`): a body stays in the world while ANY living community player keeps it
    by their own ring, view distance and drawing range (`Keeps`: the old forced and voluntary tests turned round), and is
    captured only when none does - one player's death forces nothing, nobody alive forces the capture (single player's
    old "the player died"). It is re-created near the nearest living player whose ring takes the spot in (`Reacher`), never
    within 50 m in front of any living player (`PopInSeen`), and the stuck watchdog and the route hold hop or hand off a
    body only where no player is within 40 m or looking (`UnseenByAll`). The A* grid is added where no player in the world
    is near (vanilla lays one round each). The gate uses the shortest view distance online (`GatePoint`), "near the post"
    is any community player (`PlayerNearPost`). The tick's anchor (`ScPlayers.Anchor`) still names who tells, radio lines
    and a fresh body's stamps reason from. Single player has one player, so every test reads as before.
  - **Sends:** `ScRuns.Send` and `ScMining.Send` make any player's run physical (was the host's own only: a client's and a
    dedicated server's runs were hidden). A console with no player still sends a hidden run.
  - **Escort and rescue:** "never a distress call with a player beside her" and the rescue use one rule,
    `ScRadio.RescuerNear` (now internal): the anchor first, else any living community player. The players within
    `RescueTriggerRange` of a call are told the event is on, the strongest of them sets the zombie count (`ZombieCount`
    gets that player), and they are all told when it is won.
  - **The handheld rule** (`ScRadio.RollDistress`, `ScRunnerCall`): callers pass the real player; the handheld that can
    hear a call is one the server can read - that player's when it is the server's own, else the host's own player's.
    A remote player's bag lives on their machine (the server has only a save copy, `ClientInfo.latestPlayerData`), so on
    a dedicated server a distress call or a call for help needs a powered station at home. A client's own handheld still
    decides whether that client hears the line (MP4).
  - **Call for help** (`ScRunnerCall`): no call while any living community player is within 30 m of her; the message goes
    to every community player; the marker reaches clients through `ScMarkers` (MP4) and the host's own map as before.
    It now works on a dedicated server (with a powered station).
  - **Hurt and heading home:** remote community players beside her are told in person (`scRunHurtHome`). Her give-up
    count pauses while anyone has her inventory open; a client's open talk with a runner is not known to the server (no
    talk hold for runners), so only the host's own talk pauses it.
  - **Console:** `sc runners` measures from whoever typed it (a client's own player) and says who keeps each runner;
    `sc run`, `sc run <name> recall|home|skip|offline|hurt` and `sc distress` act for the sender (they are admin test tools:
    `skip`, `offline` and `home` capture a body whoever stands beside it).
  - **No duplicate, no loss:** a runner's body is made only by `Materialize`, once per member per tick, when the record is
    offline and nothing holds the entity id (a stale copy is removed first, a stranger forces a new id), so two players near
    her cannot make two bodies; the keep rule is a union over players, so there is no hand-over moment between them. A
    capture always snapshots and saves before the removal (`GoOffline`), and a chunk the server unloads (no player's
    observer holds it any more: a client leaving, dying or teleporting away) captures through `OnEntityUnloaded` as before.
    A re-create needs the 3x3 chunks round the spot loaded and settled on the server (`Settled`), well inside the chosen
    player's observer. A re-created body keeps vanilla's pop-in rule (not within 50 m in front of a living player) for up to
    `RunPopInMaxWait` (20 s), then comes anyway, as it always did.
  - **Known gaps:** the run's dice still roll for a body forced offline beside a player (a frozen chunk, the watchdog's
    "desperate" hand-off), as for the host before. Near a client the server may hold chunks without colliders: a re-create
    there follows MP0.1's settled rule (a body brings its chunk's colliders); should one stay frozen, the frozen-body rule
    captures it again after 15 s under that rule (`RunFrozenSeconds` elsewhere) - churn, never a loss. With nobody left on a
    dedicated server the ticker stops: an online runner is captured when the server unloads her chunk or shuts down. The server could read a client's handheld from its
    save copy of their bag (`ClientInfo.latestPlayerData`), left for later (`ScHandheld` is not this part's file). MP6:
    `Observes` asks `ScPlayers.InCommunity`, so Stage 2 decides whose players keep whose runners.
- **Round MP5 (part O), outposts for every player** (`ScOutpostRisk`, `ScGarrison`, `ScOutpostPerks`, `Patches_Outposts`,
  `ConsoleCmdSc`):
  - **Already in place, checked for multiplayer and unchanged:** "the player at the outpost" is the community player nearest
    its footprint (`ScOutpostRisk.Closest`, `ScGarrison.NearestAt`, round MP0) for the attack's arrival and judging, the blood
    moon's vigil, the visit day (security, moods, the check-in), the stash and the stand-ins. A stand-in appears when any
    community player is within `OutpostStandInRange` of a loaded claim, stays while one is within 1.8 times that, and goes when
    none is; one per member (`Member.StandInId`; a body still being built is never taken for gone), and its going records her
    ammo first - the member is the record, so nothing is lost. The window's and radial's outpost commands are MP2's ops, the
    markers MP4's, outpost storage and the home link MP3's linked storage sessions: `storage.open` kind 2 has `ScHomeLink` load the home zone on the server per player, the client's view is
    virtual (the stacks come in `NetPackageScStorage`), and every change is a compare-and-set against the box, which leaves a
    box someone has open out of the view - MP3's loss and duplication table holds unchanged.
  - **A fight is sized by the players at it** (`ScOutpostRisk.GameStageAt(outpost, anchor)`): with one community player
    online, the anchor's gamestage, as before; with more, the community players (dead ones too) within `RescueTriggerRange` * 2 of the
    footprint (where a fight is judged) - one player's own, several by vanilla's party rule
    (`GameStageDefinition.CalcPartyLevel`) - and the anchor's when nobody is there (an unattended raid's size,
    `AttackerCountFor`). The raider tier follows it. The day's raider demand gate (`OutpostRaiderGamestage`) reads the highest
    gamestage of the community's players online (one online: the anchor's).
  - **One live fight at a time, per player** (`OtherFightHolds`): with one player online any live home attack or enclave
    siege holds an outpost's attack back, as before; with more, only one the player at the outpost is in (within
    `BaseAttackLeaveRange` of the zone, or `EnclaveSiegeArriveRange` * 2 of the siege's enclave). Before, a client at an
    attacked outpost while another player fought at home got nothing and the attack settled "unanswered" at its deadline.
    The other direction is not this part's: `ScBaseAttack` and `ScEncSiege` still wait for an outpost fight near any player
    (`AnyLiveNearPlayers`, `AnyLiveNear`).
  - **A stand-in's identity on a client** (`ScGarrison.SyncStandIn`): its `sc_standin` / `sc_standin_op` are set right after
    the spawn, but the spawn data a client near it gets is made inside `SpawnEntityInWorld` (NetEntityDistribution), before
    them, and the bulk cvar sync brings them seconds later - meanwhile that client's guards (no equipment window on a stand-in,
    whose items would go with it; no follower verbs) did not hold. Once per stand-in, the first second it stands with a remote
    player within 200 m, both are force-sent (after the spawn, on the same ordered channel). A client that comes near later
    reads them in its spawn data (0-SCore's NPCs write their buffs in `Write`). Nothing is sent in single player.
  - **Tells:** "attackers here", "held" (`TellAtKey`: every community player within `RescueTriggerRange` * 2) and the blood
    moon's "held by you" (`Live.BloodMoonByAll`: everyone who stood there) reach every player there, not only the nearest.
    `StartAttack`'s marker is the host's own map object whoever called the attack (remote players get theirs from ScMarkers).
  - **Raider demands:** `PayDemand` / `RefuseDemand` take an `EntityPlayer` (they took `EntityPlayerLocal`), so a request
    handler passes its sender; `sc outpost pay|refuse` act for the player who typed them. The radio window's buttons are the
    radio's own (round MP5's radio part).
  - **The stash** (`ScOutpostPerks.Unstash`) waits while `ScStorageServer.OpenBySomeone` says the claim's box is open - MP3's
    one test for every server-side writer. It writes a closed box on the server only, and no linked storage view ever shows a
    claim's own box (`ScStorage.Linkable`), so there is nothing to push: the bundles reach a player through a vanilla open.
  - **The claim gate on a client** (`Patch_CanPlaceLandProtectionBlockAt`): the game asks it only on the placing machine and
    the server never asks again, so a client gates its own claims now, from its copy (`ScMirror.Active`): an enclave's building
    (`ScRegistry.Enclaves` with the `Enclaves` setting; the copy has no `Lost`, so an enclave the server could not place this
    load still refuses its old footprint) and the claim limit with the home and outposts (`IsProtectedClaim` reads the copy's
    outposts: the server's lookup tables are never built on a client). The server's `RemoveExtraLandClaims` keeps the home and
    outposts whatever a client sends.
  - `sc outpost who`: per held outpost, every community player online, their distance and what it counts for, each staffer's
    stand-in, and an open attack's gamestage. No new op, package or localization key.
  - **Known gaps:** a client's projected haulage on the Outposts tab leaves out an outpost whose radio relay is powered while
    the home radio is silent (power is the server's: `RelayOnline` reads false on a client, and the mirror carries no relay
    state) - display only, the rollover is the server's. A claim a modified client places into an enclave's building is not
    taken back by the server. A dead player counts as present where they fell (as the one player always did in single player).
- **Round t107, forced horde lines for a remote player:** `ScHordeVoice.SayAtHome` and `SayAway` with `force` and a remote
  player (the console's sender) send the line to that player alone (`SentForced`: `FlagForced` and `FlagHordeRadio`, the
  guard's body when there is one) and queue nothing on the server. The client's `ScRadioTalk.OnRemote` skips the hearing rule
  for a forced horde line, and `RadioHeard` lets it go on the radio once out of earshot (`Line.Forced`). The package's layout
  is unchanged; an older client plays a forced line only by its normal rule. With no sender and no player of its own (a
  dedicated server's console or telnet) a forced line is not queued: the console says nobody there hears it.
- **Round MP6 (part: core), several communities on one server:** the core every later MP6 part builds on. With everyone in
  community 0, single player, a listen host and a dedicated server behave exactly as before.
  - **`CommunityState`** (`ScCommunities.cs`): one per community. It holds the `Community` record (the community's own
    fields, plus `Id` and `Name`), `Members`, `Prospects`, `Contacts`, `Outposts` and `EncRequests`. Records `Member`,
    `Prospect`, `Contact`, `Outpost` and `EnclaveRequest` carry `CommunityId`. The containers (`ScRecordTable`,
    `ScRecordList`) stamp it when a record goes in through their own indexer, `Add` or `Insert`, so it always names the
    container that holds the record. `ScCommunities` has `All` (lowest id first), `ById`, `Create(name, ownerId, ownerName)`
    (ids from the world counter), `Zero` (community 0 always exists), `AllMembers`/`AllOutposts` and `ForEach` (each
    community inside its own scope) for world loops.
  - **`ScRegistry.Current` and scopes:** `ScRegistry.Community`, `.Members`, `.Prospects`, `.Contacts`, `.Outposts` and
    `.EncRequests` are read-only properties of `Current`, so existing code reads the community in scope.
    `using (ScRegistry.Use(cid))` (also `Use(Member)`, `Use(Outpost)`, `Use(EntityPlayer)`) sets the scope until the
    block ends, exceptions included. Scopes nest. Never keep one open across a coroutine's `yield`. `Current` resolves in this
    order:
    1. the innermost scope;
    2. on a client, the mirrored community (`MirroredCommunity`, 0 for now);
    3. on a host with a local player, that player's community;
    4. else community 0.
    With one community it is always community 0, except on a client whose copy is of another community: a client's copy
    (`ApplyMirror`) is parsed into its mirrored community, so its `Current` and the community its actions send are that
    one. While two or more communities exist, an unscoped read on a server with no local player logs `[MP6 scope] ...` once
    per distinct stack trace (`sc community list` counts them). On a listen host with clients connected, unscoped reads
    are counted, and logged with `DebugLogging`. A scope still open at the start of a frame leaked: it is logged and reset.
    Main thread only.
  - **Already scoped:** `ScActions.Dispatch` scopes every op to the sender's community. The `community` int in
    `NetPackageScAction` is a staleness check only: a mismatch is refused with `scActionCommunityChanged`.
    `Allowed`/`IsOwnerOrAdmin` check the sender's own community. Also scoped:
    - the ticker (per community, see below);
    - `ConsoleCmdSc.Execute` (the sender's community, or the one `sc community use` named; a playerless console uses
      community 0);
    - the server side of `NetPackageScTalkContext` and `NetPackageScJournalReport`, and the hello.
  - **Global lookups never read the scope:** `GetMember`/`GetProspect` answer across every community (entity ids are
    world-unique). So do `CommunityOfMember`, `ScCommunities.*` and `ScPlayers.CommunityOf`. `CountStatus`, `FollowerCount`
    and `Members` stay per scope. Code that means "anyone's member" must loop over `ScCommunities.AllMembers()`, not
    `ScRegistry.Members`. A record found through a global lookup is changed in its own community: scope to it
    (`using (ScRegistry.Use(m))`) or use `RosterOf(m)`, `ProspectsOf(p)`, `RemoveMember(id)` or
    `RemoveProspectEverywhere(id)`. Removing or re-keying through `ScRegistry.Members` in another scope misses her, or
    moves her and the save writes her twice. The load keeps the first copy of a member id found in two communities.
  - **The world record:** `ScRegistry.WorldState` (`ScWorldState`). It holds:
    - every id counter (contact, outpost, enclave, infestation, enclave person, band, band person, enclave request, and the
      new community counter), so ids never collide and old journal quests stay valid;
    - the enclaves' seeding, the request clock (`EncRequestDay`), remains, refills and streamer names;
    - the world daily's own day guard;
    - the membership map.
    `Community` keeps forwarding properties for the moved fields, so call sites are unchanged.
  - **Membership:** `ScPlayers.CommunityOf(player)` reads a server map from platform id to community id. Anyone not in it is
    in community 0. On a client, its own player is in the mirrored community. `InCommunity(p, c)`, `Community(c)`,
    `TellCommunity`, `TellCommunityKey`, `OwnerOnline` and `Anchor(c)` default to the community in scope.
    `Anchor(c)` is one of that community's players: the host's own player only if they belong to it, then its owner, then a
    player at its home, then any of its players. It is null when none of them is online.
  - **The ticker** runs every per-community step once for each community with a player online, inside its scope, with its
    anchor. The world steps run once, in the first of those passes, in their old places: `ScEnclaves`, `ScEnclavePeople`,
    `ScEncDoors`, `ScBands`, `ScInfestations`, `ScStreamerNames`, `ScSleep` and `ScPanelHold`. The per-viewer pushes
    (`ScMirror`, `ScMarkers`, `ScCraftFromBase`) run in community 0's scope, as before.
    `ScDaily.Run` is a community's daily, guarded by its own `LastDay`. Where the world steps always ran, it calls
    `ScDaily.RunWorld` for every world day not run yet. That is guarded by `ScWorldState.LastDay`, so the first community
    to roll a day over runs it. A community whose players are all offline pauses: when it ticks again after other
    communities ran world days without it, its `LastDay` is rebased to the last world day run (`ScTicker.Resume`), with no
    catch-up. Whatever came due meanwhile resolves from its next rollover on. Days nobody ran (the whole server empty) run
    as before, so one community behaves exactly as before MP6.
  - **The save:** `survivor_communities.txt` (header `SCOM 2`), written atomically as before. It holds:
    - `[world]`: the world keys that used to sit in `[community]`, plus `lastday=` and `communityid=`;
    - `[membership]`: one `player=<platform id>:<cid>` line per player outside community 0;
    - one `[community]` per community, with `cid=` first and `name=`;
    - `cid=` first in each `[member]`, `[prospect]`, `[contact]`, `[outpost]` and `[encrequest]`. A record with no `cid=` is
      community 0's.
    With only the old `survivor_community.txt`, the load migrates it: its community becomes community 0 (owner kept), its
    world keys go to the world record, and every id counter keeps its value. The old file is never written again, so an
    older build still loads the state from before MP6 and never touches the new file. When the new file and its `.bak` are
    both unreadable, the old file is migrated again. When the file the load needed exists but cannot be opened at all (in
    use by another program), nothing is saved in that session (`SaveBlocked`), so the file is not replaced. A client's snapshot
    (`BuildText(true)`) is still community 0's text in the old `SCOM 1` layout (`BuildLegacyText`).
  - **`sc community`** (admin, as all of `sc`): `list`, `new <name>` (owner: the sender), `join <id> [player]`,
    `leave [player]` (back to community 0), and `use <id>|none` (the console session acts in a community until the world
    closes). The player is a name or entity id online, or a platform id. A player who changes community leaves their
    followers with the community they belong to.
  - **For modders:** read a community's state inside a scope. Entry points the core does not scope yet are marked
    `// MP6 (part <relations|network|home|gates>): ...` (Harmony, UAI, blocks, engine events and queues). A new world loop
    over "every member" uses `ScCommunities.AllMembers()`. A new id counter goes in `ScWorldState`.
- **Round MP6 (part: relations), each community its own relation to the world's groups:** an enclave's standing, known flag,
  hostility, request cooldown, thanks, gift, barter, call and reveal clocks, an infestation's known flag, and a band's known
  flag, trust and engagement are one row per (community, record). With one community nothing changes.
  - **Relation rows** (`ScRelations.cs`): `Enclave.Rels` (`EncRel`), `Infestation.Rels` (`InfRel`) and `Band.Rels`
    (`BandRel`) hold one row per community. Community 0's row always exists; another community's is made by its first write.
    The old fields are properties now (`Enclave.Standing`, `.Known`, `.TurnedHostile`, `.CallKind`, `Band.Trust` ...). They
    read and write the row of `ScRelations.Cid`: the community in scope on the server, row 0 on a client (its copy holds one
    community's view) and while a save is read. So code that runs in a community's scope is unchanged. World code that
    touches a relation opens the scope of the community concerned (the finder, the hitter, the killer: `ScRegistry.Use`), or
    walks every row (`Rels.Count`, `IdAt(i)`, `At(i)`; the day-back shifts do). `TurnedHostileAny`, `IsHostileAny` and
    `Band.EngagedAny` mean "toward any community", for a body's own behavior. `LastRecruitDay` and `RaidDay` stay the
    group's (world).
  - **The save:** a record's own keys (`standing=`, `known=` ...) are community 0's row, so a one-community save is byte for
    byte as before. Each other community's row is one `rel=` line in the same section. A community's grudges are
    `[community] grudge=<platform id>` lines. A client's snapshot writes the scope's row into the keys and no `rel=` line:
    the per-community mirror builds `BuildText(true)` inside `ScRegistry.Use(cid)`.
  - **Finding and the daily:** `ScEnclaves.Tick`, `ScInfestations.Tick` and `ScBands` (meeting, engaging) find for each
    community with a player online, in its scope (`ScRelations.ForEachOnline`). A new infestation is learned by each
    community for itself (spread from one it knew, near its home, its radio). The world daily runs the community halves of
    the relation steps (the radio reveal, the requests, allies' bundles and words) for the rolling community. Another
    community's rollover that day runs them through `ScRelations.DailyCommunity`. The request clock and the refills stay
    the world's.
  - **Requests and favors:** requests are already per community (`CommunityState.EncRequests`), one per (community,
    enclave). A siege is one at a time in the world (`ScEncSiege.OpenAnywhere`). A missing person is one search in the
    world (`ScEncSearch` looks across communities). `ScEncCalls` keys its sessions and callers by (community, enclave). A
    falling or departing group withdraws every community's request (`ScEncRequests.WithdrawEverywhere`). Each community
    courts a survivor with its own prospect under the NPC's id: `ScRegistry.OwnProspect(id)` is the scope's only (the favor
    flow uses it), `GetProspect(id)` prefers the scope's and else answers any. An enclave person's favors follow their body
    in every community. A join closes every community's favor for them.
  - **Hostility:** an enclave turns hostile toward the community in scope (`ScEnclaves.TurnHostile`; the hit, the kill and
    the threat open the attacker's scope). Its bodies carry one cvar per community: `%sc_enchostile` for community 0 (as
    before), `%sc_enchostile_<id>` for others (`ScEnclavePeople.HostileCvarFor`). The faction lookup is keyed by (community,
    enclave). `ScRelations.SideCid` finds the other side's community in O(1) on any thread: a player through a snapshot of
    the membership map, a member through `sc_member` (now community id + 1: 1 for community 0, as before), a stand-in through
    its member, a hired follower through its leader. The talk prompt, greetings and voice lines check the viewer's community
    (`IsHostileBodyTo`). Turrets and motion sensors check their owner's (`HostileNowFor`, `ScRelations.OwnerCid`). A drifter
    band holds ScTrust's per-player grudge against the players of each community it is against.
  - **Between communities:** a player's hit on another community's member or stand-in, or a killing blow, makes that
    community's people hold a grudge against the player (`ScRelations.OnMemberHit`, saved by platform id). The faction
    postfix answers 0 between them and that player (`ScRelations.GrudgePair`). `sc trust <member> [forgive]` shows or ends
    it. Another community's members can be hurt (`ScTrust.PlayerMayHurt`); your own stay under `CommunityFriendlyFire`. A
    wild survivor's grudge blocks only the hitter's community from recruiting them (`ScRelations.GrudgeBlocks` in
    `CanRecruit`), and a hit closes only the hitter's community's favor (`ScTrust.CloseFavourForCommunity`).
  - **For modders:** read or write a relation inside the scope of the community it is about. A new relation field goes in
    `EncRel`/`InfRel`/`BandRel` with a property on the record, and in the `rel=` line (`ScRelations`).
- **Round MP6 (part: network), what each client receives:** each client gets its own community's state. With one community
  every byte is as before, apart from the state package's version byte and community id.
  - **The copy** (`ScMirror`): the push groups its clients by `ScPlayers.CommunityOf(ClientInfo)` (the server's map, never a
    number the client sent). For each community it builds the snapshot (`ScRegistry.BuildText(true, cid)`, i.e.
    `BuildClientText`) and the derived block (`BuildDerived(cid, ...)`) inside that community's scope. Each community keeps
    its own last text and last block for the "nothing changed, send nothing" comparison. A client whose community changed
    gets its new community's whole snapshot at the next tick. `NetPackageScState` is layout version 2: a version byte, the
    flags, then the community id. The client sets `ScRegistry.MirroredCommunity` from it before `ApplyMirror` parses the copy
    into that community, inside its scope, and drops a derived block of another community.
  - **World records in a copy** carry that community's relation values: the snapshot is built inside `Use(cid)`, so
    `WriteEnclave`, `WriteInfestation` and `WriteBand` read `standing=`, `known=`, `turned=`, the dated stamps and `trust=`
    through the records as that community sees them (the fields today, its rows once relations are per community). With two
    or more communities, an enclave the community has not found goes as its footprint only (`WriteEnclaveFootprint`: id,
    name, building, size, prefab, `known=0`): what the client's claim gate and its refusal tips read. With one community the
    text is `BuildLegacyText`'s, byte for byte.
  - **The derived block** is built in the community's scope, so `beds`, `extrabeds`, `allied`, `nests` and `defhorde`
    (`ScPlayers.Anchor(cid)`) are its own. With two or more communities it also lists the players online outside community
    0 (`pcom=entityid:cid,...`). A client's `ScPlayers.CommunityOf(otherPlayer)` reads that list (`ScMirror.PlayerCommunity`).
  - **Markers** (`ScMarkers`): one published set per community. Every `CollectMarkers` runs in that community's scope
    (`ScRunnerCall` skips another community's runners). `NetPackageScMarkers`' community is the recipient's own. A client
    keeps one community's set: a whole set replaces it, and changes to another community's set are dropped. The listen
    host's own map follows only its own community's set (`HostFollow` runs in the host's scope). A subsystem's own
    `AddMarker` for the host should take `ScMarkers.HostOwner()`: the host's player when they are in the community in
    scope, else null.
  - **Recipients:** Craft from Storage's open boxes are per community home (`ScCraftFromBase.ServerTick`). A rescue quest goes
    to its runner's community (`ScQuestNet.Remotes(m.CommunityId)`). A re-keyed enclave person's favour goes to every remote
    player, since each community courting them holds its own quest. A journal report is answered against the sender's own
    community's prospects and runners. `ScVoice.SendCommunity(cid, line)` sends a runner's radio line to her own community.
    An enclave falling and an infestation cleared tell each community that knew them (the relations part's own news; the
    network part's `ScWorldNews` was dropped at the merge).
  - The ticker no longer runs the three per-viewer pushes in community 0's scope (the core's note above): each push scopes
    what it reads per community.
- **Round MP6 (part: H), the home's machinery per community:** each community's home runs on its own. With one community
  nothing changes.
  - **`ScHomeState<T>`** (`ScHomeState.cs`): a side table with one `T` per community, made on first use. `Current` is the row
    of the community in scope, `For(cid)` any community's, `Rows()`/`ForEach` every row made so far, `Reset()` all of them.
    Community 0's row is a plain field (no lookup). The home systems keep what used to be their single-instance statics in a
    private row class, and the old static names are now properties that read `Current`'s row. So the code that uses them is
    unchanged and reads the community in scope. It is a side table rather than fields on `CommunityState` because a client
    replaces its `CommunityState` objects with every snapshot, and each system's `Reset` clears its whole table (every
    community's row) as it cleared its statics; `ScMorale`'s HUD clock and morale override never had a reset. `ScHomeTimer` is a ticker step's throttle (one float). `ScHomeScope.Of(entity)` scopes to the member a body is
    (a stand-in's member too), and `ScHomeScope.Of(cid)` to a community; both do nothing with one community.
  - **Per community now:** the home attack's session (`ScBaseAttack`: attackers, zone, clocks), the blood-moon siege
    (`ScSiege`), noise (`ScNoise`: counters, steady part, clocks), the watch's clocks (`ScWatch`) and watch fatigue's last
    terms, the home scan's session (`ScScan`: last run, settling chunks, shelter seeds, `LastChunks`/`LastLoaded`/
    `LastMissing`/`LastMs`), the shelter cache (`ScShelter`), the night lease's bed group (`ScSleep.HomeGroup`: bed counts
    and grants per home), the home hold (`ScHomeHold`), intruders and responders (`ScInvestigate`), the doors' owner
    (`ScDoors`), the activities' clocks (`ScActivities`), Lights out's check, the away news's clock (`ScDefence`), salvage's
    noted horde (`ScSalvage`), the followers' opened Water (`ScDrinks`), the morale HUD's clock, Bond's clocks and
    blood-moon roll call (`ScBond`), the near-home nests list (`ScInfestations.NearHome`), and the throttle of every ticker
    step that had one static timer (also `ScAmmo`, `ScRadio`, `ScMining`, `ScOutposts.Tick`, `ScGarrison`, `ScScouting`,
    `ScOutpostRisk` with its blood-moon flag, `ScMissing`).
  - **Position to community:** `ScZone.CommunityAt(pos)` is the community whose home zone holds a position (-1 for none),
    and `ScZone.ContainsFor(cid, pos)` tests one community's zone. Neither reads the scope. A heard sound counts for the
    community whose zone it lands in. A Watch post placed belongs to the zone's community, and one removed to the community
    whose list holds it. A member opens doors by her own community's owner. `UAI.ScArea.HomeOf(cid)` is each community's home
    zone for the AI; `ScHome.Of` gives a resident her own community's, which reads that zone whatever is in scope.
  - **The ticker:** `ScSleep.Tick(w, player, world)` runs in every community's pass (its members against its own beds), the
    enclaves' people once. `ScShelter.Step` (every frame, before the ticker's scopes) steps each community's cache in its
    scope. `ScHomeHold.Tick` holds every community's home in one pass, each in its scope, so a home is let go after its
    players log off. `ScHomeLink` steps every link in its own community's scope, once a frame. `ScDoors` refreshes each
    community's owner in its pass and shuts doors once a frame. `ScBond` drains every community's events and recomputes the
    morale of each community that changed.
  - **A community that pauses** (none of its players online, the user's decision 9): the ticker lets a live attack's
    attackers go in the first pass the community has no player online (`ScBaseAttack.ReleasePaused`, from the pass's online
    list, never from a clock); when it ticks again the fight resumes as after a reload. Its noise does not integrate the hours
    it was away (the noise clock is rebased), and while paused it hears nothing (`ScNoise` skips a community not online in
    the pass: `ScTicker.OnlineNow`). Its world-time stamps move on by the time it missed (`ScTicker.PauseShift`, from
    `Community.SeenTime`, the world time it last ticked, saved as `seentime=` only while there are two or more communities;
    a pause under one game hour, `PauseShiftMin`, is not shifted):
    the home attack's arrival, the defenders' fight, a live fight's start, the gap to the next call (`ScBaseAttack.ShiftPaused`),
    a guard's watch shift (`ScWatch.ShiftPaused`) and an outpost fight's deadline (`ScOutpostRisk.ShiftPaused`). A blood
    moon's roll call (`ScBond.OnResumed`) and an older blood moon's home mark (`ScSiege.OnResumed`) go. All only with two
    or more communities. A new piece of state that runs on world time and must not run while a community is paused shifts
    in `PauseShift`.
  - **For modders:** a new piece of home state goes in a `ScHomeState<T>` row, never a plain static. Code that has a body or a
    position and no scope scopes itself (`ScHomeScope.Of`, `ScZone.CommunityAt`). The reads the AI makes with no entity
    (`ScBaseAttack.WideDefence`/`InDefenceArea`, `ScInvestigate.InArea`, `ScShelter.InZone`) follow the scope the UAI entry
    points set, and 0-XNPCCore's close-range tick (`Patches_Xnpc` X5: `ScTeam.Recount`, `ScRetreat.Update`, `ScPosture.Update`),
    which runs from the avatar controller's own `Update`, opens the member's scope itself.
- **Round MP6 (part: G), entry points and gates:** the engine's entry points act for the right community, and another
  community's members, stations and ground are not yours (the user's decisions 3 and 4). With one community every test
  below answers as before MP6. `ScGates.cs` holds the helpers:
  - **Whose body, whose ground:** `ScGates.MemberCommunityOf(npc)` is the server's record (a stand-in's by the member id it
    carries, `MemberOf`), else the synced `sc_member` cvar, which holds community id + 1 on a member (1 = community 0, as
    before). A client has only the cvar and its own community (`ScPlayers.CommunityOf(local)` = `MirroredCommunity`).
    `IsOwnMember(npc, player)` / `IsForeignMember(npc, player)` compare it with the player's. `CommunityAt(pos)` is the first
    community whose home zone holds the position, else the owner of the held outpost there; `OutsiderAt(pos, player)` and
    `OtherCommunityIn(min, max, own)` / `OtherCommunityInClaim(claim, own)` (another community's home bounds or outposts
    touching a box) are the gates. `OwnMember(id)` is `GetMember` limited to the community in scope - an action handler's
    sender's.
  - **Scopes for entry points:** `ScopeOf(npc)` (her community; nothing for a stranger, who reads no community),
    `ScopeOf(memberId)`, `ScopeOfPlayer(p)`, `ScopeOfActor(entity)` (a killer: a player's or a member's), `ScopeAt(pos)` and
    `ScopeAt(pos, placer)` (a block: the position's community, else the placer's), `ScopeOfUser(platformId)` (server only).
    Each returns `default(ScScope)` with one community, so it costs a count test there. `PushHeld`/`PopHeld` keep a scope a
    Harmony prefix opens until its finalizer. `Patches_Gates.Patch_UaiScope` opens the NPC's scope around vanilla's
    `UAIBase.Update(Context)` (priority first, closed in a finalizer), so every SC consideration and task, and every hook a
    UAI task reaches (0-XNPCCore's craft, loot and book tasks into `Patches_Craft`), reads her community. Also scoped: a
    member's and a stand-in's death (`Patches_Npc.Patch_SetDead`), a kill's skill XP (the killer), `OnHire`, the runner
