    unload delegate and shutdown, `ScWork.End` (her community, whoever ends it), the book and station-sweep patches,
    `ScArms`' swaps, `ScMedic`'s tick, the door opener, `ScRunnerCall.OnHit`, the radio, watch post and guard tower blocks,
    the claim radial and the claim-limit patches, bundle opening and an infested room's clear (the nearest player).
  - **Gates:** the talk's member rows read `ScOwnMember` (replacing `NPCHasCVarSDX sc_member value="1"`, which would read
    community 0's members only once the cvar is id + 1); `ScForeignMember value="not"` hides 0-XNPCCore's inventory,
    Equipment and Jobs and gear rows and Head home from an outsider; `ScFollowerVerb` refuses another community's member.
    No talk hold (`ScWork.CanHold`, `HoldForRemote`, `HandOver`), no member panel (`XUiC_ScMemberInvGroup.IsMember` is the
    viewer's community; `IsForeign` + `RefuseForeign` refuse SCore's own screen too, handing a client's copy back), no
    equipment window (`ScEquipRedirect`), no gear changes (`ScMemberEquip.Gate`), no spoken follow or dismiss, and every
    member action naming another community's member acts on nobody (`OwnMember`). `CanRecruit` already refuses anyone's
    member (the core's global `GetMember`). `home.set` and `ScOutposts.CanEstablish` refuse another community's home or
    outposts; the packing station (`ScActionsStock.StationFor`), the radio station (`ScActionsRadio.WhyNotStation`, and the
    host's own "Use radio") refuse outsiders. Linked storage needs no gate: every view is the sender's own home or outpost.
    Another community's member is not protected from your shots (`Patches_Npc.Patch_FriendlyFire`); her community's grudge
    is the relations part's.
  - **World loops** read every community: the seeding exclusions (`ScGates.AddTakenPois`), the favor POI pick, the
    door-path test, Bond on a kill, portraits kept, work flames, outpost stand-in orphans, runners at load and shutdown,
    the sleeper re-arm (`ScQuests.IsOurPoi`), mining spots, run destinations and radio contacts' buildings. The outpost index
    (`ScOutposts.Rebuild`: `IsOutpostClaim`, `IsOutpostPoiKey`, `BoxHitsOutpost`) is the union of every community's outposts
    ("anyone's outpost"); `AtClaim`, `ByPoi` and `OutpostAt` stay the community in scope's. Console finders by name still
    search the console's community (`sc community use <id>` for another).
  - **Queues** keep their community: a queued Outpost Claim Block (its placer's, established in that community's tick pass)
    and a withdrawal's refund ticket. **Recipients:** `ScGates.LocalPlayerOf(cid)` replaces the "host's own player" fallback
    where server code picks a recipient (stationing, a leader to restore, a shift's owner, a runner's call, Leadership, a
    craft or book star, a distress call's end): the host's own player only when of that community, else its anchor.
    `ScPlayers.Nearest` takes a community filter.
  - **Not gated on the server:** SCore's own inventory screen for another community's member is refused by the opening
    machine (`Patches_MemberInv`); SCore's remote session on the server is not checked, so only an honest client is stopped.
- **Round t2 (storage, upgrades, revive, cornered):**
  - `Patch_CraftFromBase` sits on 0-SCore's three-argument `RemoteCraftingUtils.GetTileEntities(EntityAlive, float, bool)`, which
    every count and take goes through (crafting with `forRepairs` false; an upgrade's or repair's count with false, its take with
    true), so the boxes taken from are the boxes counted. It removes boxes the server says are open (a client), boxes locked
    against the player (`ScStorageServer.LockedAgainst`: vanilla's `IsUserAllowed`, so a debug-menu client passes as it does
    when opening one), on the host boxes someone has open, and another community's boxes (`ScGates.ForeignBox`: on its ground
    or owned by one of its players - the server reads its own map, a client the derived block's `fgnd`, every other community's
    home rectangles and held outposts' footprints, and `fown`, whose box owners are another community's; both only with two
    or more communities), then adds the home's linked boxes inside the home zone (0-SCore's enemy rule by `forRepairs`).
    Round MP7 (review U): `fown` lists `ScMirror.OwnerHash` values (the first 8 bytes of SHA-256 of a platform id, as hex),
    never the ids, and a client hashes a box lock's owner the same way before it compares (`ScMirror.ForeignOwner`).
    0-SCore's drop box (`AddToNearbyContainer`) keeps its own range (`Patch_CraftFromBaseDropBox`).
  - `UpgradeRepairFromStorage` (default true) answers true for 0-SCore's `BlockUpgradeRepair`/`ReadFromContainers` in its config
    read (`Patch_UpgradeRepairFromStorage` on `Configuration.CheckFeatureStatus(string, string, bool)`). 0-SCore's repair code
    then takes from storage; its own flaws (a partial count accepted, the bag counted twice in a take) are 0-SCore's.
  - Another community's member: `HiddenRingEntry` leaves Voice Control's entries on while she is down (`ScDowned.IsDown`), so
    anyone may revive her; nothing on the server stops a revive.
  - The base's own boxes (`Fill`) get the same tests as 0-SCore's near list (open on the host, another community's box).
  - Left as they are: when the boxes change between an upgrade's count and its take, 0-SCore's take can fall short and the
    upgrade's materials are lost (0-SCore's own repair code; reported upstream by the user); the storage changes are not under
    `SC_V34` (the 3.2 build is another branch); while another community's member is down every Voice Control entry is left on,
    but Voice Control offers only Revive on a downed NPC.
  - `ScHealBackOff`: cornered at or below `CorneredHealBelow` with `ScRetreat.GunReady` and the pursuer within 2.5 m, she keeps
    shooting (`ShootsInstead`) instead of starting a heal a hit would cut short - only at a pursuer she can see (vanilla's
    `EntityAlive.CanSee`), and for at most 10 s from the wait's first such verdict.
- **Round hr1 (run to heal at home, safe rooms, last stand; all server-side, members at home only - round hl1's `AtHome`,
  the home zone + `ResidentRoamZoneMargin`; followers away from home keep the old rules):**
  - `ScSafeRoom` (new): a 3D breadth-first fill from her feet and head cells through every cell vanilla's
    `Block.IsMovementBlocked` lets through (closed doors/hatches, glass, bars and fences are walls), all six neighbors, at
    most `MaxCells` (512) cells, every cell inside the home zone + margin and under a roof (`World.IsOpenSkyAbove`), in loaded
    chunks. Safe = enclosed, no enemy (awake or asleep) in a room cell or the cell above one, no armed enemy (a ranged weapon
    in hand) that can see her within 25 m, none within 2 m that can see her. `SafeToHeal` caches it 1 s per body and logs a
    changed verdict; `FillFrom(..., doorsAsWalls)` is shared with `ScShelter`; `sc saferoom [id|name]`.
  - The heal order (`ScHealBackOff.StartWalk`, region Home): a safe room heals where she stands; else k1's straight point;
    else a walk into an enclosable room within 30 m (`ScShelter.PickRoom`: a scan candidate in a room, 2.5 m from every door,
    a walk that never takes her nearer the threat; `ScDoors.TrackPassed` shuts the doors she passes; heal on arrival if
    `SafeToHeal`); else a flight point 8-20 m away on 12 directions (a standable floor inside the base, 4 m farther from
    the threat, no enemy within 5 m) - both walks are checked on the navigator's real path (`CheckPath`: it must reach the
    spot and pass no enemy, else the spot is set aside 10 s); else 0-XNPCCore's roll; else the cornered rules (nc1's D2) and
    `NoRoom`, which the last stand reads (`NoRoomNow`).
  - The roll (step 5): 0-XNPCCore's public `SdcsRollProbe.ProbeForHeal` and `AvatarSdcsNpcController.StartForcedRoll`, bound by
    name (`XnpcRoll`; missing: step 5 off with one warning), only with an enemy within 2 m and a landing spot (5 m along the
    heading) inside the base, at most once per 8 s. Not its heal sequence: X15 still skips `SdcsSelfHealScan` and nothing adds
    `SdcsIsHealing`; the roll leaves a "heal now" verdict for the wait, and `ScMedic.TryHeal` keeps the wait fresh while
    0-XNPCCore's busy flag is up for it (`ScHealBackOff.Rolling`).
  - MedicMaxWait at home counts from the wait's first walk (`ScHealBackOff.TimeoutAt`/`WalkClock`: the walk's length - the path's
    once known - over `RunnerRunMps` + 2 s, later walks may push it out, at most 12 s after the first began); before any walk
    and away from home, `MedicMaxWait` from the wait's start as before.
  - The gap rule (`ScHealBackOff.FightsInstead`, t2's `ShootsInstead` widened at home): no use while an enemy within 2.5 m can see
    her (or she it) and she has a usable weapon (her pair's gun with rounds, or her melee weapon); no 10 s cap. `ScMedic.GapHolds`
    applies it at home on every path - the back-off's verdicts, the timeout, sheltering, on watch, a Stay - logging once per wait.
  - `ScMedic`: a member's or stand-in's use lasts at most `MemberUseSeconds` (1.5 s) from the use trigger (3P_Bandage_Use is about
    1.4 s; the use still ends when that clip leaves its layer first); the finish line (verbose) gives the trigger time, whether
    the hold pose showed, and what ended it. In a last stand no cloth bandage for a bleed.
  - `ScShelter`: after its seeds each scan runs a room pass - one `FillFrom` with doors as walls per new space, at most one fill a
    frame - giving each candidate a room id (`Cand.Room`: 1+, -1 none, 0 not checked) with the room's cell count and door list
    (`RoomInfo`). `PickIn` scores an enclosable room 8 better and 3 worse per door standing open now beyond the one she shuts.
  - The last stand (`ScPosture.UpdateStand`, X5 tick, in scope): at home, a fight posture or SHELTER, 2+ enemies after her within
    3 m (or overwhelmed), and `NoRoomNow` (or sheltering with nothing to heal with on fallback (c)); ends 1.5 s after that stops
    holding. Its start hands the shot a member-wide 4 s beat (`ScBackOff.HandToShot`); in a stand `MarkNoPoint` and the gain test
    keep it member-wide for 4 s and `Blocked` has no reload pass. `ScArms.StandHand` owns her hand (not on watch/Stay or against
    your weapon order): the pair gun's class (`GunClass`: magazine 5+ close, 2 or less slow, 3-4 by `Reload_time` 2.5 s - only
    there, since vanilla gives every shotgun 2.6 s), a 6 s commitment on the one hand clock (`ScPosture.HandClockAllows` holds
    `StandCommitSeconds` in a stand with an enemy in reach) broken by a dry gun or a refused swing, melee in 0-XNPCCore's bout,
    and a refusal (its bout exit with the target in reach, or no swing within `NoSwingWindow` - 1 s plus two swings at the
    weapon's `AttacksPerMinute`, at least 2.5 s) is member-wide for the 6 s: no per-target refusal, no R2 rest. R2's own no-swing
    exit outside a stand uses the same weapon-sized window. Logged: the stand's start (with the weapon class) and end, each
    commit, each refusal.
  - `ScDowned`: `sc down` marks the member (`consoleDowns`); the Down record it makes takes the mark (`Down.Console`) and its
    revive (`Up`) sets 0-XNPCCore's `SdcsDownedRedown` back to 0 the way 0-XNPCCore sets it (synced). `sc downed` counts DOWN and
    FRAGILE members, names who can revive a downed one, and marks a test down.
  - `RegenCapShare` defaults to 0.7. No new Harmony targets, no `ScNet.Protocol` change, no new localization.
  - The review's fixes: a MedicMaxWait timeout the gap rule holds calls `ScHealBackOff.TimedOutHeld` - the walk ends, she
    fights on (`FightOnWait`; after a timeout only the gap rule holds her, not D2's line), no more walks in that wait
    (`TimedOutWait`), and the "no room" verdict is set for the stand; room and flight walks are capped at `MaxWalksPerWait`
    (4) per wait. At a room spot reached through a door she waits up to `ArriveWaitSeconds` (3 s, never past the walk's
    deadline) testing afresh every 0.5 s (`ScSafeRoom.SafeToHeal(e, fresh, ...)`; the 1 s cache answers only for the same
    feet cell) for `ScDoors` to shut it, leaving at once when an enemy is inside. `BeginWalk` drops the navigator's old path and
    `CheckPath` skips it (`StalePath`) and waits while `PathFinderThread.IsCalculatingPath`; it also sets aside a path that
    leaves the base. A stand holds through the item's cooldown (`NoRoomRecently`, 15 s, while she is at or under the heal line).
    A close gun out of her hand is drawn again while its stored magazine is loaded (`StoredRounds`: `ScAmmo.StoredMagazine` or
    0-XNPCCore's magazine table cvar). The gap rule also covers "nothing after her within reach" at home, the safe room's near
    test is 2.5 m while she has a usable weapon, and `FightsInstead` is cached 0.25 s per body. Verdict keys carry the kind,
    not the enemy's id.
  - The re-check's fixes: the wait at a room spot ends at once when the pursuer is within `ArriveLeaveDist` (3 m) or the gap
    rule holds (asked every tick: with the door open the room test sees no enemy), and "leave" is that test, not the verdict's
    key; the wait has its own 3 s (the walk's deadline is pushed to it, never past 12 s after the first walk). The gap hold
    where nothing is after her (`ScMedic.GapOnlyHolds`) starts no reach wait - ScHealBackOff stays out and MedicMaxWait is
    not used up; a reach wait on then ends. A walk counts toward `MaxWalksPerWait` once its path passes `CheckPath`; failed
    paths have their own cap (`MaxFailedWalksPerWait`, 8). The stand's hold also runs from her last use's start and while an
    item still heals her (`ScMedic.UsedOrWorking`). `ScHealBackOff.Forget` drops a body's state in `ScLooks.Forget`.
- **Round MP6 (review fixes):** the four reviews' fixes on the merged build. What a modder must know:
  - **The listen host's own markers** are drawn for the host's own player in the host's own community: the periodic and
    restore paths (`ScEnclaves`/`ScInfestations`' `RefreshMarkers`, `ScBands.UpdateMarkers`) take `ScCommunity.LocalPlayer`,
    never the world pass's anchor. The one-shot restores of community records' markers (`ScRadio`, `ScScouting`,
    `ScOutpostRisk`, `ScOutposts`) remember the community they drew for (`markersFor`) and redraw, after clearing every
    marker they drew, when the host's community changes. A new host marker follows the same rule.
  - **A world step that acts on a community's record** opens that record's scope: a missing person's body, death and unload
    (`ScEncSearch`, the search's community), an enclave person's death and the new leader's news (every community that knows
    the group, `ScEnclavePeople.ForKnowing`). The "in use" target sets walk every community (`ScEncRequests.AddTargets`).
  - **Per community now:** the talk facts' favor (`ScTalkContext.Build` reads `OwnProspect`), journal reports' favors
    (`ScQuestNet`, keyed by community and prospect), enlisted enclave people (`ScEnclaveDeals.EnlistedOf`/`EnlistedCount`:
    one per ally per community, counted against that community's follower limit), a band seen (`ScEnclavePeople.SeenByCommunity`),
    Bond for a kill, light sleepers' tells. One missing person at a time is checked at the offer and again at the accept
    (`ScEncSearch.SearchOpen`). The claim limit keeps any community's home (`ScGates.IsAnyHome`); `sc home set` and
    AutoHomeFirstClaim never take another community's ground.
  - **A player who changes community** (`sc community join/leave`): their old community's accepted requests are withdrawn,
    their talk holds and work shifts end (`ScWork.ForgetPlayer`), their new community's grudge against them is dropped, and
    the ticker stations any follower whose live leader is not of her community (`ScTicker.Relink`). A future join flow
    (MP7) should go through the same steps.
  - **Left open after the reviews:** a paused community's siege or accepted request holds that enclave for everyone until it
    is back; SCore's inventory screen is refused on the opening machine only (above); every save makes each online
    community's copy be rebuilt and compared (one dirty flag for the world); a client's `ScRelations.PlayerCid` answers its
    own community for every player although `pcom` lists the others; a joiner whose snapshot is too big keeps its old copy
    until the next save; a band's `NextAssert` is one for the band, so only the first community's engagement asserts; a
    client's `ScTrust.TrustOf` reads `sc_favourDone`, which any community's finished favor sets; the door opener's scope
    fallback for a body that is no member (`ScDoors.OpenerCommunity`) is unused (only members and stand-ins reach it).
- **Round MP7 (core), community modes and a player's own relations:** the host's `CommunityMode` (Shared, PerPlayer,
  PerParty) and `MaxCommunities` (0 = no limit) in `ConfigSurvivorCommunity`. In Shared mode nothing changes, except that
  moving the home never changes the owner any more (in every mode). `ScMembership.cs` holds the mode, personal records,
  founding, the merge, `Move` and `ScOwnerRule`.
  - **The mode** is the config's until the save's first community record starts (`ScMembership.LockMode`): in Shared the
    first home start (`ScZone.SetHome` or `EnsureHome`'s adoption when it starts one, or `sc community new`); in
    PerPlayer/PerParty the first personal record or founding. From then on it is the save's, `[world] mode=`
    (`ScWorldState.Mode`, `ModeLocked`, `ModeWritten`), and a config that differs is logged once per load. A save with no
    `mode=` whose community is going (a home, an owner, members, anything it holds, several communities, players listed) is a
    save from before MP7: Shared, written nowhere, so its text stays byte for byte as before. `sc community mode
    <PerPlayer|PerParty> confirm` switches such a save once (`SwitchMode`): every community keeps its owner, who is now listed
    in the map; an accepted request whose acceptor is in none of them passes to that community's owner; every other player
    gets a personal record. A player online who is moved out is told so and loses their talk holds, shifts, favor quests,
    followers and links, as in `Move`. `ScMembership.Separate` means PerPlayer or PerParty on the server.
  - **Personal records** (PerPlayer/PerParty): a player in no founded community has one, a real `CommunityState` with
    `Personal` set (`[community] personal=1`), owned by that player, with no home. It is made on the server's first ask for
    an online player (`ScPlayers.CommunityOf` calls `EnsurePersonal`) and at spawn (`OnPlayerSpawned`). Community 0 is the
    first player's personal record while it has no owner, home or members, so single player and the first founder keep id 0.
    Later players get new ids from the world counter. Ids are never reused: a merged record retires as a one-line tombstone,
    `[world] retired=N` (`ScWorldState.Retired`). Community 0 is never retired: a merge empties it (`ScCommunities.ResetZero`)
    and the next player with no record claims it. The membership map lists community 0's players in these modes (a Shared
    save never writes such a line). An owner is always in their own community: `CommunityOfId` answers a founder's community
    for an unlisted owner (`FoundedBy`).
  - **No community is -1** (`ScPlayers.NoCommunity`), and it never falls back to community 0. `CommunityOfId` answers it in
    PerPlayer/PerParty for an offline player with no record, and `CommunityOf` answers it when the server cannot tell.
    `ScPlayers.CurrentCommunity` is no longer -1, so `Community(-1)`, `InCommunity(p, -1)`, `Anchor(-1)` and `Nearest(..., -1)`
    mean nobody. In these modes a scope of -1, or of a community that is gone, reads `ScCommunities.None`: an empty record with
    id -1 that is never listed, saved or mirrored, whose relation rows are throwaway (`RelRows.Get` of a negative id). The
    same goes for `ScCommunities.OrNone`, a client's snapshot of a missing community (`BuildClientText`), the mirror (no copy
    for a client in none), a placer in none (`ScGates.ScopeAt`) and the faction lookups' shortcuts (`ScRelations`).
  - **Founding:** `community.start` (access `Anyone`) is "Start a community here" (the radial asks it through
    `ScActionsClaim.RequestHome`). `ScMembership.Start` does what `home.set` mode 1 did in Shared (the Owner rule, refused once
    a home exists). In PerPlayer/PerParty it turns the sender's personal record into the community: `Personal` false, the
    home set, the owner already the founder. It answers `scMp7MaxCommunities` once `MaxCommunities` founded communities exist
    (`IsFounded`; personal records never count). `ScZone.SetHome` and `EnsureHome` write the owner only when they start a
    community that has none. `home.set` mode 1 still starts one in Shared.
  - **Move** (`ScMembership.Move(platformId, toCid, reason, who)`) is the one way a player changes community: the console,
    `community.leave`, and later the ally and party hooks (`ByAlly`, `ByAllyRemoved`, `ByParty`, `ByPartyLeft`). It runs:
    1. the mover's talk holds and work shifts end, and so do their links (`EndLinks`: the enclave fighter they enlisted heads
       home, their home link, linked-storage view and the server's panel hold end);
    2. leaving a founded community, the requests they accepted pass to that community's owner, else to another of its
       players online (`ScEncRequests.HandOver`: the quest leaves their journal and goes to the heir's), else are withdrawn
       with no standing lost; their favor quests close in their journal while the favors stay the community's, no longer
       "taken on" when nobody else of it holds one (no server op starts a favor quest in another player's journal);
    3. their followers go home to the old community;
    4. from a personal record, the merge, then the retire; to none, a fresh personal record (community 0 in Shared);
    5. the new community's grudge against them ends;
    6. tells and a save.
    In PerPlayer/PerParty a founded community's owner cannot be moved out, and nobody joins a personal record.
    `community.leave` (Community access) refuses the owner, a personal record, and community 0 in Shared mode.
  - **The merge** (`Merge`): favors and requests move. Where both sides court the same survivor or hold a request from the
    same group, the one further along stays (done over taken on over offered, accepted over offered; the community's on a
    tie) and the other closes with a tell; the favors' "held by a report" marks follow (`ScQuestNet.RemapFavours`). Contacts
    move, and the stockpile adds up. For each relation row the record has: enclave standing takes the higher, known is OR
    with the earlier day, the community's hostility is kept (a war it earned never ends because a player joined; the record's
    own hostility is dropped), thanks owed kept; infestation known is OR; band known is OR and trust takes the higher. The
    community's own request, gift, barter, call and reveal clocks stay its own. Wild survivors' grudges against the joining
    player are forgiven on the bodies that are loaded. The retire then drops the record's rows and clears its hostility cvars.
  - **Permissions** (`ScActions.WhyNot`): `Anyone` passes. A personal record's player may use only the relation ops
    (`ScActions.PersonalOps`: wild survivors' favors, the enclave talk's gifts, requests, threats, the missing survivor's
    search and escort, sending an enlisted person home, a bundle opened, a withdrawal's refund). Every other Community op and
    every Owner op answers `scNoCommunity`. Talk, trade and markers need no op. The Owner rule (`ScOwnerRule.May`) is
    `SharedOwnerOnly` in Shared, the owner and admins in PerPlayer, and in PerParty also
    `ScOwnerRule.PartyLeaderMayAct(player, cid)` while the owner is offline (round MP7 (part AP), below).
  - **The ticker** runs a personal record as any community with its player online. Its relation steps run, its home steps
    skip by their own tests (no home, members, outposts or radio), and its pass carries the world steps when it is the
    first online.
  - **Held rewards** (what a favor or request pays that needs a community, for a player with only their own relations).
    Each is kept on the personal record, so founding turns it on in place, and a merge moves it:
    - a wild survivor's finished favor: the prospect stays done, and "Join us" opens once they have a home, with no new favor.
      The supplies hand-over no longer needs a home for them; the dialog's favor rows are part U's;
    - an enclave person's finished favor: the same, and recruiting answers `scNoCommunity` until then;
    - allies' daily bundles: they go into the record's stockpile (no packing station until a home), and add up in a merge;
    - an alliance's radio perks (radio barter, the fighter and supply-drop calls, siege calls) need the radio, which needs a
      home: the standing that earns them is kept;
    - enlisting an ally's fighter, recruiting one of an enclave's people, a whole enclave joining, a band's "come to our
      place": refused with `scNoCommunity`, and the standing or trust that opens them is kept;
    - what a request or favor pays at once (standing, a Leadership event, an infestation made known, thanks owed, the
      threat's bundles in the bag) is paid as for anyone.
  - **`sc community`:** `list` shows the mode (the save's and the config's), marks `[personal]` records, lists retired ids and
    counts founded communities. `join` merges a personal record. `leave` means community 0 in Shared and a fresh personal
    record otherwise. `new` moves its owner in, in PerPlayer/PerParty. `owner <id> <player>` hands a community over, moving
    the player in first. `mode <PerPlayer|PerParty> confirm` is the switch.
  - **Core review fixes** (`review_mp7_core.md`):
    - **Pruning at load** (m1): in PerPlayer/PerParty, `OnRegistryLoaded` drops every personal record but community 0 that
      holds nothing (`HoldsNothing`: no home, members, outposts, favors, requests, contacts, grudges, radio or tower state,
      stock or saved relation row) and unmaps it, logged once with the count. No tombstone: the world's id counter already
      keeps the id from coming back. The player gets a fresh record at the next spawn. `EnsurePersonal` maps a record whose
      `OwnerId` is the player back to them when the map line was lost (n4).
    - **`EndLinks`** (m2) runs in `Move` and for each player the mode switch moves out (m4). The switch also hands accepted
      requests over through `ScEncRequests.HandOver` with the owner online (else they wait with `AcceptedBy` = the owner),
      tells the player (`scMp7LostAccess`) and frees favors no player online still holds.
    - **A handed-over missing-survivor request** (m3): `ScEncSearch.StopEscortOf` stops the survivor following the leaver
      first (they wait where they stand), so the heir's quest leads there.
    - **Started and claimable** (m5): a community that holds anything counts as going at load (`AnyCommunityStarted`), and
      community 0 is claimable as a first player's record only while it has no owner, holds nothing and lists nobody.
    - **Merge rules:** an enclave person's favor also clashes with the community's favor for that person under another key
      (m6); a favor the merge drops while the mover is offline closes at their next journal report
      (`ScQuestNet.CloseFavourAtNextReport`, m8; `ReleaseFavours` reads their last report through `ReportedFavour`); of two
      accepted requests of one kind from one group the one at the later stage stays (n7). Each favor and request step and the
      contacts and stock step run in their own try/catch: a step that throws moves its item as it is, and the summary says so
      (m10). `Move`'s catch names the step it reached.
    - **No community has no hostile cvar** (m7): `ScEnclavePeople.HostileCvarFor` of a negative id is `%sc_enchostile_none`,
      which no body carries; `OnTurnedHostile` in that scope only rebuilds.
    - **This machine's own player with no platform id** (m9) is `ScPlayers.LocalKey` ("local") in PerPlayer/PerParty, so
      single player has its one record; a player with no id yet is logged once per entity.
    - **Smaller ones:** `AutoHomeFirstClaim` adopts nothing for a personal record, said once at load (m11); offline names come
      from the persistent player list (`NameOf`, n2); `CommunityState.Personal` counts only in PerPlayer/PerParty, and Shared's
      Start clears it (n3); `sc community list` from a playerless console says which community it acts in (n6); `sc community
      owner 0 <player>` gives out a free personal community 0, and `new` clears the owner when moving them in fails (n8).
  - **For modders:** change a player's community only through `ScMembership.Move`. Treat -1 as no community. Check
    `CommunityState.Personal` before anything that needs a founded community. Later parts plug in at the markers
    `// MP7 (part A)` (the allies reconcile at load, at spawn, at founding and at the switch; Leave's opt-out; how a player
    joined), `// MP7 (part P)` (`PartyLeaderMayAct`, party members joining at founding, the party postfixes calling `Move`) and
    `// MP7 (part U)` (the derived block's `mode=`, `role=` and `cpl=`, the window's no-community page and Leave button, the
    radial's role gating, the talk's `scNoCommunity` and favor rows).
- **Round MP7 (part F), follower limits per player** (`ScFollowers`; the user's decision 9): `MaxFollowers` is each player's
  own limit in every community mode, Shared included; `MaxMembers` stays per community. `ScFollowers.Count(player)` is the
  members following that player (`Following` or `PendingStation`) across every community's roster, matched by
  `Member.LeaderId` (the leader's platform id, written by `Follow` and kept by the ticker's `NoteLeader`), plus the enclave
  people that player enlisted. A follower with no leader id (a save from before MP0) counts for her live leader while her body
  is loaded, else for whoever `ScTicker.LeaderToRestore` would give her to (the host's own player of her community, nobody on
  a dedicated server). `EncPerson.EnlistedById` holds the enlister's platform id beside the entity id `EnlistedBy`: set by
  `ScEnclaveDeals.EnlistBody` (paid enlistments and radio fighters), cleared with it, saved as `enlistedid=` after `enlisted=`
  only while enlisted and never to a client (a load sends every enlisted person home, so it matters only to a save written
  mid-enlistment). A record with no platform id falls back to the entity id while that player is online. `ScFollowers.Full`
  gates `ScCommunity.Follow`, `ScEnclaveDeals.EnlistBlocked` and the radio fighter (`ScEncCalls.FollowersFull`, both in
  `CanCall`, for the player at the radio, and on arrival, for the caller); `ScFollowers.Line` feeds `sc party` and
  `sc status`. Always pass the player who asked (the action's sender), never the host's own player. Single player
  (`OfflineServer`) keeps the old count (`ScRegistry.FollowerCount` + `ScEnclaveDeals.EnlistedCount`, the community's): its
  one player leads every follower whatever leader id a save recorded. In Shared the community as a whole can now have more
  followers out at once (one limit per player online).
- **Round MP7 (part AP), land claim allies and parties:** `ScAlliesParties.cs` brings players in and out by the user's
  decisions 6 and 7, always through `ScMembership.Move`. Shared mode never runs any of it; single player has no ally or
  party event, and its reconciles find no ally.
  - **How a player joined** (`ScJoins`, PerPlayer/PerParty): `Move` calls `ScJoins.Noted`, which records `admin`, `ally`
    (with the founder whose ally link brought them in) or `party` for a player who joins a founded community they do not own
    (`ScWorldState.JoinedHow`, `JoinedVia`; saved as `[membership] joined=pid:how[:link]`). An ally or party change only ever
    takes out an `ally` or `party` player: an admin's join, and a player in a community from before part AP or the mode
    switch (no record), are kept. Leave sticks: leaving a founded community by Leave or by an admin's move opts the player
    out of it (`ScWorldState.OptOuts`, `optout=pid:cid`). The allies reconcile never pulls them back in until their ally link
    with its owner is removed and made again; another player's party invite never does until they accept an invite
    themselves (or start a party). Ids in these lines are escaped (`%`, `:`, `=`, `;`, line breaks as `%XX`). A save without
    them writes nothing new.
  - **PerPlayer** (`ScAllies`): `PersistentPlayerList.OnPersistentAllyChangeEvent` (static, subscribed once in `InitMod`,
    acted on only on the server; vanilla raises it twice per change, once per side, after the store changed, never at load).
    Now allies: when one side founded a community and the other is in none (own relations, or no record while offline),
    the other joins it, merged (`Made`). An ally of two founders joins the first. No longer allies: an ally-joined player of
    the community whose owner (or whose recorded link) this was loses access, unless still allied with its owner or their
    link (`Gone`). So an admin's hand-over (`sc community owner`) does not drop the old owner's allies. After a loss the
    reconcile runs for them at once, so another founder's ally joins that community. The reconcile (`Reconcile`: one player;
    `ReconcileAll`: every listed player and every ally of a founded community's owner, offline included; the lowest
    community id wins) runs at load (owed by `OnRegistryLoaded`, run at `GameStartDone` or the first spawn, since the game
    holds the ally store only after the players.xml read that loads the registry), at each spawn, at founding (the founder's
    allies in none join), at the mode switch (before its requests pass, so an owner's ally keeps their accepted requests) and
    right after a player leaves a founded community (`QueueReconcile`, on the main thread's next pass). The store is read
    through `ScAllies.AllyView` (platform id strings), which an offline harness can fill.
  - **PerParty** (`ScParties`): Harmony postfixes on `Party.ServerHandleAcceptInvite`, `ServerHandleAutoJoinParty`,
    `ServerHandleLeaveParty`, `ServerHandleKickParty` and `ServerHandleChangeLead`, never `ServerHandleDisconnectParty` (a disconnect changes nothing;
    vanilla parties are session-only, the saved membership map stays the truth). The host's own players list calls the same
    statics, so a listen host is covered; single player never reaches them. Accept (`Accepted`): the party's community is the
    inviter's founded community, else the only one among the members, else the leader's, else the lowest id. Every member in
    none joins it unless they left it. A member in a founded community keeps it; when it is the invited player, both sides
    are told. Leave or kick (`Left`, the member list taken in a prefix): the player loses access when they joined by party
    and the party they left held another of their community's players, never the owner. Founding pulls in the founder's
    party members in none. The `AutoParty` game setting's join (`AutoJoined`) counts as an accept with no inviter, except
    that it never ends a Leave opt-out (it happens at every login, not by the player's choice). `ScOwnerRule.PartyLeaderMayAct` is the helper rule: the leader of a party holding one of the
    community's players, of the community themselves, while its owner is offline (checked inside, so the network's role key
    can ask it as is). A new leader who now acts for the owner is told (`scMp7PartyHelper`).
  - **Left open:** a kick is trusted as vanilla trusts it (the server never checks that the kicker leads the party).
- **Round MP7 (part U), what players see of the community modes** (`ScCommunityView`, `XUiC_ScCommunityPlayers.cs`, the
  generator, `Config/dialogs.xml`). Shared mode with one community sends and shows exactly what it did before.
  - **The derived block** (protocol 9 from the merge; `NetPackageScState`'s layout is unchanged): each community's block
    gains `mode=` (PerPlayer / PerParty; absent means Shared) and `cpl=`, the community's players as
    `role:online:entityid:name` entries (`o` owner, `h` helper, `m` member; the entity id is -1 offline; the name escapes
    `% , : ;` and line breaks; never a platform id). Round MP7 (review U): at most 200 (`MaxListed`: the owner, a helper and
    those online first), and `cplt=` with the true count when that cut any. Each client's copy of the block is followed by its own
    `role=` (owner, helper, member or personal) and `ownops=1` when `ScOwnerRule.May` lets it use the Owner ops
    (`ScCommunityView.ReceiverLines`, from the client's platform id and body on the server). A client whose own lines change
    while its community's block does not (an admin, a new party leader) gets the block again (`ScMirror.SendChangedRoles`).
    Round MP7 (review U): a client whose hello came before the server had its body gets `ownops=` from the parts of the
    owner rule that need no body (the owner, a community with no owner, Shared without `SharedOwnerOnly`, an admin by the
    client's permission level), and its lines again at its spawn (`ScMirror.OnPlayerSpawned`, from ModApi's spawn event).
    These keys are written only while `ScCommunityView.KeysOn`: PerPlayer/PerParty, or two or more communities. The players
    list comes from the membership map, the players online in the community and its owner (always in their own community);
    Shared mode's community 0 lists its online players and owner only. Offline players are named from the game's player
    list. A helper is PerParty's party leader while the owner is offline (`ScOwnerRule.PartyLeaderMayAct`, part AP's).
  - **The client** parses `cpl=` once per block (`ScMirror.CommunityPlayers`) and reads `mode=`, `role=` and `ownops=`
    through `ScMirror.Str`; `ScMirror.Reset` clears them at world start and shutdown, a disconnect included.
    `ScCommunityView.Take` gives the window and the radial one answer on every machine: the registry on the host and in
    single player, the block on a client. Its `View` says `Personal` (the no-community page), `Gated` (PerPlayer/PerParty,
    a founded community, and the Owner ops are not this player's), `ShowPlayers` (the list: the keys are on, never in single
    player) and `CanLeave` (not the owner, not own relations, not community 0 in Shared mode, and a platform id to send it
    with: `HasId`). `PlayerTotal` is `cplt=`, or the list's count.
  - **The window:** a PLAYERS button in the header (`btnPlayers`) swaps the morale panel for the players list (name, role,
    online, the player's own role in words) with **Leave community** under it (`btnLeaveCommunity`, two presses,
    `community.leave`). A player with only their own relations sees the no-community page in place of the three panels:
    how to start one ("Start a community here" on their claim's radial) and how to join one in this mode (a land claim
    ally of an owner, or a community player's party), what they can do now, the enclaves they know with their standing,
    and their favors and enclave requests; Open storage hides for them. Ask to leave, Disband and an Owner facility action
    (rationing) are grayed when `Gated`; a grayed button still asks, so the server (`ScActions.WhyNot`) judges a role that
    changed a moment ago. Tools/generate_community_ui.py draws all of it in both looks; new control ids only, the old ones
    kept. Round MP7 (review U): the players list (9 rows) and the no-community page's enclaves, favors and requests page
    (Showing X of Y and a pager, as the member list does), so every one can be seen; requests taken on come before offered
    ones. Leave's hint goes when its five seconds are over or the panel turns.
  - **The land claim radial** (`TEFeatureSCCommunity.Allow`): "Set as community home", "Disband outpost" and "Start a
    community here" (for a founded community that has no home yet) hide when `Gated`; "Linked storage" hides for a player
    with only their own relations. "Start a community here" for them asks `community.start` (the core's `RequestHome`).
  - **The talk:** for a player with only their own relations, the talk facts' recruit verdict is false with the reason
    `scNoCommunity` when the survivor would otherwise join (`ScTalkContext.Build`; the host's `ScCanRecruit` answers the
    same), and her favor is rolled for them. `ScNoCommunityFavour` (that reason on a client, `CanRecruit` plus a personal
    record on the host) opens their favor rows in `Config/dialogs.xml`: "Is there anything I can do for you?", the same
    Mark it / Sure, I'll see what I can find / Actually, I've already cleared that rows, a supplies hand-over with no join
    (`ScTakeFavourItems` alone), and once it is done a row whose answer says she comes when they have a home. "Join us"
    (`scPoiDone`, `scSuppliesDone`) opens at founding, as the held reward says. Round MP7 (review U): their hand-over ends the
    talk, as the community's own does, and her thanks come from the server's answer (`ScTakeFavourItems` value `held`:
    `scMp7FavourThanks`), so a refusal is never thanked first; their "Actually, I've already cleared that." has its own reply
    (`scMp7PoiClearedSt`) instead of the join's. The talk's "Ask to leave" hides where the window grays its button
    (`ScOwnerOrders`: the view is not `Gated`).

## How it hangs together (for modders)

- NPCs are 0-XNPCCore's `EntityAliveSDXV4` survivors (SCore's legacy `EntityAliveSDX` still works for pack NPCs). A resident is SCore's *FarmHere* shape: `Leader`/`Owner` cvars
  cleared, `hired_<id>` removed from the player, leader cache evicted, `buffOrderGuard` with the guard
  position set to the post, `Persist=1`, static spawner source, plus the `SCResident` utility-AI package
  added at runtime (`Config/utilityai.xml`). A follower is a plain SCore hire; the order it holds (`CurrentOrder`) is
  read as a member state (`ScOrders`), never repaired unless it is one nothing gives a follower (None, Hire, Task).
- The recruit dialog is injected by xpath into **every** dialog that has a `Hire` response, so character
  packs join the pool without being listed. The custom operators (`ScCanRecruit`, `ScHasHome`, `ScFavour`,
  `ScFavourDone`, `ScFavourQuestOpen`, `ScHasFavourItems`, `ScFavourPoiCleared`, `ScFavourBrief`, `ScStartPoiQuest`,
  `ScStartSuppliesQuest`, `ScTakeFavourItems`, `ScJoin`)
  live in `SurvivorCommunity.dll`.
- NPC cvars (`sc_member`, `sc_owner`, `sc_cardio` ...) mirror the registry so XML can read them; a cvar of
  exactly 0 is never saved by the game, so "false" is always -1 or absent.
- Harmony targets: every one is listed in `ModApi.ExpectedTargets` and checked at startup (`patch verification:
  all 111 targets patched` with Voice Control loaded, 97 without (the 3.4 build: 126 and 108); a `MISSING PATCH` line names one that did not
  bind). Among them
  `PersistentPlayerList.ReadXML/Write(string)` (registry load/save), the SCore NPC classes' `SetDead` (deaths) and
  `PostInit` (re-pin the spawner source), `SleeperEventData.Update` (no POI re-arm for favour quests, outposts and
  enclaves), `SleeperVolume.UpdatePlayerTouched` (no sleepers in an enclave's building; an infested building's count,
  spawn points and special), `SleeperVolume.UpdateSpawn`, `Spawn` and `ClearedUpdate` (an infested room's spawn step,
  its special, and the clear check; the feral share rides the `EntityGroups.GetRandomFromGroupList` postfix; the
  zombie-cap fix rides UpdateSpawn and UpdatePlayerTouched),
  `TEFeatureStorage.OnBlockActivated` (an enclave's containers), `SpawnManagerBiomes.SpawnUpdate` and
  `World.GetRandomSpawnPositionInAreaMinMaxToPlayers` (no wild NPCs on an enclave's grounds, with the group-pick
  postfix), the two turrets'
  and the motion sensor's target filters and `EntityAliveSDXV4.HandleNavObject` (raiders), SCore's two NPC
  inventory packages (`NetPackageHarvestInventoryRequest` / `...Update`: a remote player's open window),
  `EntityAliveSDXV4.UpdateWeapon(ItemValue, bool)` (a gun put away keeps its rounds), and `EntityAliveSDXV4.Write` /
  `Read` (the gun in hand is saved after SCore's own data, as `SCM1` + the item, and put back when the NPC loads; an
  older SurvivorCommunity or SCore alone skips those bytes).
- Locked & Loaded is reached by name only (`ScMags.cs`: `ModManager.ModLoaded("LockedAndLoaded")`, then
  `REInvMags.NpcMagazines`' `Arm`, `Refill`, `Rounds`, `Record`, `Restore` and `Carries`, bound with their exact
  signatures once per game); there is no reference to its DLL. Missing or throwing, it is switched off for the game
  with one log line, and SC's NPCs use loose rounds.

## The land claim block's model (`Resources/SCLandClaim.unity3d`)

SC replaces vanilla `keystoneBlock`'s `Model` with its own: a desk carrying a lit lamp and the claim ledger, standing
beside a tall steel locker. The geometry is four of the game's own block models — `cntLockersTallMixClosed`,
`officeDesk`, `deskLampLight02Black` and `cntBookPile08` — extracted from the 3.4 bundles and baked into one mesh.

**Nothing of that art is in this repo.** It is The Fun Pimps' geometry and this repo is public, so `.gitignore` keeps
out `Resources/SCLandClaim.unity3d`, `UIAtlases/ItemIconAtlas/scLandClaim.png` and `art/landclaim/preview_*.png`. A
fresh clone therefore has a claim block with no model until the bundle is built. What IS here is everything needed to
rebuild it: `art/landclaim/BuildScLandClaim.cs` (the Unity editor script) and `Tools/build_landclaim_bundle.py` (the
driver — copies the inputs into the shared Unity project, checks the tag list, runs Unity in batch mode, verifies the
result with UnityPy, renders the icon, and copies the bundle into `Resources/`).

Its inputs — the FBX and the textures — live outside the repo, by default in
`F:\Game Related Programs\Creation Stuff\SC_LandClaim_assets\unity_in` (`--inputs` overrides it). The folder above it
holds the extraction and the working files, with a README giving the derivation of the placement; `make_maps.py` there
rebuilds `unity_in` from them.

    python -I Tools/build_landclaim_bundle.py           # build, check, copy into Resources/
    python -I Tools/build_landclaim_bundle.py --no-copy  # build and check only

Block-side facts worth keeping:

- **3 x 2 x 1 cells.** The mesh stands on the cell floor and is centred on its parent cell, which is the middle of the
  bottom row, so the block needs `ModelOffset 0,0,0` — the game's default is `0,0.5,0` and its own models are sunk half
  a block to match it. Placement works out as `world = P + (0.5, 0, 0.5)`; `BlockShapeModelEntity.GetRotatedOffset` and
  its `Chunk.cs` caller are the authority.
- **No collider on the prefab root.** The game takes a box or capsule there as the block's own bounds, and on a block of
  many cells every cell gets that one box. The colliders sit on a tagged `Collision` child carrying
  `RootTransformRefParent`, as vanilla's own 2-wide `deskOfficePrefab` does.
- **DMG0 and DMG1 must both exist.** `keystoneBlock` carries `MeshDamage "DMG0,7000,DMG1,1,"` and
  `BlockShapeModelEntity.UpdateDamageState` calls `transform.Find(name).gameObject` with no null check, so a prefab
  missing either throws the moment the block is damaged. `Transform.Find` only looks at direct children. There is no
  broken version of this model, so both groups hold the same meshes.
- **Winding, not just normals.** Every step that negates an axis mirrors the mesh and reverses what triangle winding
  means. Unity culls by winding, so a mirror without a winding reversal renders the whole model inside-out — you see
  through the locker doors and into the lamp shade. Blender does not cull backfaces, so it looks perfect there right up
  until it reaches the game. The driver measures it (`winding_agrees`) and refuses to copy a bundle below 90%.
- The vanilla `_rmom` maps are repacked by `make_maps.py` into one Standard-shader mask each (R metallic, G occlusion,
  A smoothness). The `_rmom` channel meaning is **inferred** from materials whose physics are known, not read off the
  game's shader; if it is wrong, metallic reads ~0 and the model just looks a little flat.
